- **Read `/tmp/ralph_verify_3.txt` and `_4.txt` FIRST**: both read `no run for a3a1ea2… on 'main'; pushing… fatal: unable to access … Failed to connect to github.com port 443 after 31s` / `could not push main (check auth/network)`. No test verdict was ever reached. Transport, not code — for the FIFTH and SIXTH time on these two stories.
- **PLAN before editing**: (1) re-run each stored verifyCommand in a retry loop classified by the script's OWN verdict line, (2) count the FULL junit-results artifact separately since `ci_verify.sh` only greps the FILTERED tests, (3) restore the two stories by `git checkout`, not by hand, (4) close AUDIT-TOOL: run its stored verifyCommand, run `audit_check.sh`, then prove AC3 with a real branch + real CI run, (5) only then edit the PRD.
- **WORKSPACE-CORE**: attempt 1 transport (`could not push main`), attempt 2 reached the verdict line — **EXIT=0, 73 matched test(s) all passed** on run 36788191042. (The retry fix from `f705f25` earned its keep immediately: attempt 1 of 5 downloads died `Could not resolve host: productionresultssa17.blob.core.windows.net` and attempt 2 landed.)
- **CONTINUITY-LAW**: **EXIT=0, 53 matched test(s) all passed** on the same run, attempt 1.
- **Full suite, checked separately**: `junit-results` artifact of run 36788191042, downloaded independently and proven complete with `unzip -t` before counting: **1309 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped across 180 XMLs** — identical to the count four prior iterations recorded, so nothing regressed while the audit story was built.
- **Restoration**: `git checkout -- .ralph/prd.json`, then `cmp` against `git show HEAD:.ralph/prd.json` — IDENTICAL. No Kotlin touched in either story.
- **AUDIT-TOOL**: stored verifyCommand **EXIT=0**. `audit_check.sh` — **all 22 checks PASS**: AC1 (detector names none of the three disclosed deviations; audit.py never extracts Section 12; found independently `ClaimKind`, `ChangeLayer`, `DEFAULT_MIN_EVIDENCE_CLAIMS`, `DEFAULT_MIN_INTERVAL_MS`, and the three-way classification named as `forTarget`; every deviation cites file+line; the report states Section 12 was not read), AC2 (Workspace.kt + InMemoryWorkspace.kt → 0 deviations, and ≥1 observation with a stated reason for the declined near-miss), AC4 (no AST-level merge/push-main/prd.json/reject_story path; `--make-branch main` refused; `--run-ci` outside `audit/` refused before any push; a full run that BUILDS a branch leaves HEAD, branch and prd.json byte-identical), AC5 (5+ structural deviations with `model_calls == 0`; exactly 1 call for 1 naming candidate; 3 candidates still cost 1 call), AC6 (self-audit non-vacuous — 12 findings about itself, and it states what that pass cannot see).
- **AC3 got its real proof**: branch `audit/continuity-threshold` (commit 36ae9b6, worktree removed, nothing merged), pushed, then `CIV_BRANCH=audit/continuity-threshold bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '*'` → run **36791635610**, conclusion success, **1309 matched test(s), all passed, EXIT=0**. The FULL suite on a branch of its own, unmerged. The patch reconfigures the flat SLOW gate into a function of the field's own accepted-change history, both curves saturating at a stated ceiling, and at zero prior accepted changes behaviour is byte-identical to the flat gate.
- **Pre-existing failures observed (not in scope):** none. 1309/1309 on main; 1309/1309 on the audit branch.
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - `vision_audit.sh` with `--make-branch`/`--run-ci` needs an EXPLICIT `--base`/`--head` whenever HEAD is not the newest production-Kotlin commit. With no arguments on a docs-only HEAD it resolves `base = HEAD~1`, finds nothing, prints `no changed production file … nothing to audit` — and still **exits 0**, which reads as success while auditing nothing.
  - The full-suite artifact must be picked BY NAME. The run carries `debug-apk` too, and `artifacts[0]` was the 27MB APK, not `junit-results`; unzipping it yields 0 XMLs and a confident, completely fake "0 tests" reading. Always print name+id before downloading.
  - A rejection log that ends at `could not push …(check auth/network)` with no verdict line is worth a retry, but so is one that ends at a verdict line: check which one you have BEFORE assuming.
  - Restoring an auto-rejected story is `git checkout -- .ralph/prd.json` + `cmp` against `git show HEAD:…`. Never hand-edit the three things the harness write changed (`passes`, the `| AUTO-REJECTED` suffix, the dropped trailing newline).
  - prd.json `notes` stays the LAST field, closing quote present, no trailing comma, trailing newline at EOF — verified with `python3 -c "import json; json.load(...)"` plus `tail -c 3 | cat -A` this time.

## Sun Oct 4 2026 - ONE-1-VISION-SYNC
Thread: (local iteration)
- Story 7 of the Stage-4 "One Friday" PRD: `.ralph/VISION.md` brought in line with Venon's stated vision. Docs-only - one markdown file, no Kotlin, no gradle, no network.
- All ten passages (H, P2, P3, P4, P6, P7, P8, B1, B2, B3) are in place at the places the story names, and are VERBATIM: they were extracted programmatically out of the story's own `description` in prd.json rather than retyped, so the wording cannot drift from what Venon wrote. Two blocks of existing text were replaced (the old title+intro, and Section 1's "This file is not a demand" paragraph); nothing else was reworded. Full changed-line ranges are in the story's `notes` (AC4).
- AC2 proven structurally, not by eye: the edit script compared Sections 4 and 13 before/after using audit.py's OWN extractor regex and refused to write on any difference; the git diff contains no hunk in either section. A second guard walked every original line and confirmed none was lost beyond the two replaced blocks.
- AC3: the exact stored verifyCommand exits 0. `bash .ralph/vision_audit.sh` still parses Sections 4 and 13 out of the edited file and prints its Headline, and its report is byte-identical to the pre-edit report except the `Generated <timestamp>` line - 5 deviations, 4 observations, 0 model calls, same findings, same patch. That equality is the proof the edit changed nothing the audit tool reads. `.ralph/audit_check.sh` also still passes every AC (AC1/AC2/AC4/AC5/AC6), including its proof that audit.py extracts Section 12 zero times, so the three new Section 12 bullets remain disclosures the tool cannot read.
- Files changed: `.ralph/VISION.md` (this story), plus carried-over uncommitted work from the previous iteration that is committed here so it is not lost: `.ralph/prd.json` (AUDIT-TOOL passes:true + its AC notes + this new 12-story Stage-4 PRD, with ONE-1-VISION-SYNC passes:true + notes), `.ralph/REPO_FACTS.md` (the 2026-10-01 AUDIT-TOOL section), `.ralph/progress.txt` (the AUDIT-TOOL entry plus this one), `.ralph/audit/audit_6be9f660c0.md` (regenerated by AC3; timestamp line only).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The passages are written as ONE unwrapped line each. That is deliberate: the story's own grep targets (`legacy scaffolding, quarantined`, `Two compositions exist`, `replay of the original episode text blocked`, `seed of the final FRIDAY`) are substrings that hard-wrapping would split across a line break and silently break the stored verifyCommand forever. Markdown renders identically. Do not re-wrap them.
  - Story passages in a prd.json `description` are delimited by `' ` (quote + SPACE), not by `' . ` - there is no sentence-ending period after the closing quote. Apostrophes INSIDE a passage (`Venon's`) also make naive quote-pair parsing wrong. And the passage order in the description is not the section order: P4 is followed by PASSAGE P6, only P8 is followed by PASSAGES B1-B3.
  - A non-greedy `'(.*?)'\s+NEXT` regex anchored on the wrong next-passage label does not fail loudly - it silently swallows the two following passages (and their instruction text) into one 2159-char "passage". The cheap guards that caught it: assert the extracted text contains no `PASSAGE`, assert it ends with `.`, and assert it appears in the destination file exactly once. Two of the three greedy passages then looked "duplicated" in the file, which is what exposed it.
  - `bash .ralph/audit_check.sh` is the cheap regression oracle for any VISION.md edit: it proves Sections 4 and 13 still parse and that Section 12 is still structurally unread, with no network and no gradle. Run it after touching the file, not just `vision_audit.sh`.
  - The audit report under `.ralph/audit/` is regenerated (timestamp only) by every `vision_audit.sh` run, including the harness's own re-verification of a stored verifyCommand - expect it to be dirty after verification and do not read that diff as a finding change.

## Codebase Patterns
- For Kotlin-code stories in this GitHub-CI-backed repo, the stored verifyCommand = an optional static grep clause + `bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '<NewTestClass>.*'` class filters (exit 0 iff >=1 JUnit case matched and none failed in the CI junit-results artifact). The harness runs it after passes:true, so it pushes HEAD and polls the real CI run; filter mode ignores unrelated baseline failures. Local gradle stays banned.
- An auto-reject is only evidence about the CODE if the captured verify output (/tmp/ralph_verify_*.txt) shows a real test failure. `ci_verify.sh: GITHUB_TOKEN is not set` there is a HARNESS env failure — reproduce the true verdict by hand with the token set before debugging code. When `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` already equals HEAD and a completed run exists for that SHA, `bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '<filter>'` reuses it instantly (no push, no 25-min wait).
- Fixtures driven through the REAL CognitiveEngine path must avoid the `it`/`that`/`this` REFERENT-unknown substrings (even inside words like "favorite") and the VAGUE_ACTION verbs (do/make/handle/fix/take care of/deal with), or doubt hits 1.0 and the reasoning organ wakes unpredictably in otherwise-deterministic tests.
- TermuxJarvisServer IS the JVM-executable production composition root (mirrors JarvisEngine.init, discloses its JVM-safe doubles InMemoryGraph/DeterministicEmbedding in main source). For any 'exact production call path' AC proof of a JarvisEngine.init-wired seam, construct TermuxJarvisServer(port=0, backendOverride=FakeModelBackend()) and drive its engine through a recording LatencyPipeline with `bridgeSend = { captured.add(it) }` — the captured text IS the outgoing generation request payload (IdentityInChatPipelineGroundTruthTest, DialectIntegrationTest pattern). The only honest exception to 'swap only the 2 Android-bound stores': the last hop (modelManager.send) may be a recording collector, never an assertion on model reply text.
- CognitiveEngine nullable seams are added AFTER capabilityFabric and default null; each carries a KDoc 'Null keeps the pre-X path byte-for-byte'. Adding a seam = constructor param + read in processInput + (optionally) JarvisEngine.init + TermuxJarvisServer wiring + JarvisOrganGraph node+edge + SystemGraphTest pin.
- CONTINUITY-GATE-ENFORCED-SEAM (2026-09-14): per-turn generation signals now ride a single typed ContinuityGate (com.jarvis.app.continuity). All future per-turn payload signals should be added as new Snapshot fields inside ContinuityGate.snapshotForTurn, then surfaced through IdentityContext.gatherForTurn(snapshot) / ContextWindowAssembler.assembleFrom(snapshot) — the assembly hops accept the Snapshot ONLY, never raw signals.
- Custom Gradle `Test`/`JavaExec` tasks (verifyLiveOllama, runTermuxJarvisServer) must wire `classpath` from compile output DIRECTORY entries (`files(mainOut, testOut, runtimeCp)`); `files(...get().asFileTree)` expands to per-`.class`-file entries a JVM classloader cannot resolve (ClassNotFoundException). Task-level `filter.excludeTestsMatching` combines with command-line `--tests` (test runs iff matches an include AND no exclude), so class excludes reliably pull live tests out of wildcard sweeps. Kill processes by exact name (`pkill -x ollama`), never `pkill -f '<str>'`, when that string exists in the harness's own cmdline.
- prd.json `verifyCommand` fields are eval'd VERBATIM by ralph.sh (`eval "$VCMD"`). Never append result annotations, `->`, or parentheses to that field — any shell metacharacter becomes a syntax error and auto-rejects the story. Verification results go in `notes` / progress.txt only.
- Re-verifying an already-green full-sweep verifyCommand can silently return `testDebugUnitTest UP-TO-DATE` (cached-output reuse from a prior same-filter run) — that proves nothing. Force a genuine re-run by prefixing `:mobile:app:cleanTestDebugUnitTest` (deletes only test-result outputs; compiles stay up-to-date), then confirm the log shows the test task among the executed tasks and EXIT=0.
- JarvisEngine.init is the SINGLE production composition point: construct the real subsystems there and fill CognitiveEngine's nullable seams (blendedRetriever/graphStore/identityContext/capabilityFabric). It is an Android-bound `object` (Context/HandlerThread/Robolectric-unusable-on-aarch64) so can't be constructed in unit tests — prove wiring on the JVM by replicating init()'s composition in a harness that swaps ONLY the 2 Android-bound stores (FakeMemoryGraphStore, TestEmbeddingProvider) for JVM references, and drive the turn through the REAL LatencyPipeline -> CognitiveEngine.process. Every other object must be the real production class.
- PRODUCTION WIRING IS THIN: `JarvisEngine.init` passes only 5 of CognitiveEngine's 16 constructor params (scope/memoryStore/humanCore/modelManager/cognitiveAdmissionPolicy). A story marked `passes:true` proves the subsystem + tests are green, NOT that it is wired into the live turn. Before trusting a wiring claim, grep `JarvisEngine.kt` for the seam being filled; the only live advanced seam today is CognitiveAdmissionPolicy → ModelManager.wake.
- The one source-of-truth organ map (SystemGraph) is built ONLY inside SystemGraphTest.buildJarvisSystemGraph, is never instantiated in main, and is itself stale/incomplete (omits failure/body/humancore/voice/research/cloud/resolution/android; points safety.approvalMechanism at a non-existent class). Treat WiringDiagnostics output as the map's declaration, then cross-check with a production grep.
- Pre-existing-failure lists decay: the 6 CapabilityExecutorTest failures and memory.* native-download failures (long documented since BIBLE-STEP-1) are now RESOLVED on the current tree (only cognitive.planning GoalPlanner/CognitivePlanningIntegrationTest replan failures remain). Re-run before re-asserting any historical failure list.
- JUnit4 test methods must be void: `fun x() = runBlocking {...}` infers `Unit?` when the block tail is a safe call (`stored?.let{...}`) → InvalidTestClassError "should be void"; always write `runBlocking<Unit> {...}`.
- Kotlin override functions CANNOT declare default parameter values — the interface default only applies when the static type is the interface; call concrete typed overrides with all args.
- Construct singletons in `JarvisEngine.init` on the handler thread or main coroutine scope.
- `CognitiveEngine` takes `MemoryStorePort` (implemented by `MemoryStore`) and `HumanCore`.
- `MemoryStorePort` is a single-method interface (`queryMemories`) — test fakes only need that one method.
- Kotlin `@Volatile var property: Type?` automatically generates a `getProperty()` JVM getter, so defining explicit `fun getProperty()` causes a platform declaration clash.
- In coroutine Flow tests, collect events into a thread-safe list (`Collections.synchronizedList`) and await all expected events before cancelling the collector job.
- Set timeout >= 600000ms for initial Gradle Kotlin compilation on Termux/Android.
- When `EvolutionEngine` gates all candidates as REJECT, invoke `SelfRepairLoop` (if injected) for the diagnose/repair cycle.
- `SelfRepairLoop.repair()` takes `RepairRequirement`, a `suiteFilter` string, and a `verifier` lambda; use `""` for suiteFilter to match all Gradle XML results.
- Kotlin test backtick method names cannot contain `/` — use spaces instead.
- Gate 2 (SandboxExecutor) runs in `SelfRepairLoop.conclude()` BEFORE `PromotionGate.decide()` — the sandbox stress test tests the repaired `AlgorithmSpec` against adversarial inputs via `ResourceSandbox`, independently of Gradle verification results. `SelfRepairLoop` takes an optional `sandboxExecutor: SandboxExecutor?` constructor param; when null the sandbox gate is skipped (backward-compatible).
- `TopicTracker` uses Jaccard similarity over keyword sets with stopwords removed; boundary detection skips the turn immediately after a boundary fires to let the window accumulate turns in the new segment.
- Galaxy Memory tests use `FakeMemoryGraphStore` (in ConsolidationDaemonTest.kt) which stamps `validFrom` with real wall-clock time and whose `addFact` defaults salience to 0.5. Use `addFakeNode(MemoryNode(...salience=...))` to insert nodes with explicit salience/validFrom for decay tests.
- `MemoryImportanceScorer.decayedStrength` floors to `salienceFloor` for any node with `salience >= salienceFloor`. Under a short `baseHalfLifeMs` + large elapsed time, high AND low salience both floor to the same value (indistinguishable) — pick a baseHalfLifeMs large enough that the simulated elapsed time keeps both computed strengths above the floor.
- On this Termux device, `com.jarvis.app.memory.*` wildcard fails (EmbeddingProviderTest: 2 sqlite-vec .so download; MemoryGraphStoreTest: 7 xerial sqlite-jdbc native-lib). Scope verifyCommands to specific passing test classes.
- The ToolBuilder (Builder stack, §15) is constructed ONLY through `com.jarvis.app.builder.ToolBuilderComposition.build(...)` — one production composition point used by both JarvisEngine.init (via hosted requestBuild) and every test harness, so the stack cannot drift between production and tests. The builder must consume UniversalResearchEngine output through `researchPersist` (all wired channels → the store), carrying the received `MechanismCandidate`s on BuildResult.researchCandidates, never the local-only `research()` stub.
- A `ControlBackend` id MUST be a member of `DeviceControlRouter.ORDER` ("official-api"/"intent"/"media-session"/"accessibility"/"shizuku"/"adb") — the router sorts by `ORDER.indexOf(id)`, so any other id gets index -1 and is tried BEFORE every rung, silently destroying the documented fallback order the moment a second backend exists. ScreenBridgeControlBackend.id is "shizuku" (its true rung).
- New device-control backends: put ALL Android-runtime interaction behind an injectable port interface (e.g. `MediaSessionControlPort`) with a production `Platform...` impl that is a compile-time-only android.jar reference (device-only, like PlatformBiometricPromptResultMapper) — the backend then runs fully on the JVM against a deterministic fake port. `execute()` must return VERIFIED only when a re-read of platform state observably confirms the dispatch (never fire-and-assume).
- The process-global `HumanCore` singleton is mutated by any test that calls `HumanCore.init` + `OwnerBiometricBinder(HumanCoreOwnerBindingPort()).bind(...)` (PhaseBDisconnectedSubsystemWiringTest and OwnerBiometricBindingTest both do). Because JUnit class order is unstable, a later test asserting a FRESH `Bound` outcome can see `ReAuthenticated` and fail. New harnesses that only need "biometric authority signal from a real owner store" should bind through an isolated `HumanCoreGraph` over FileStorage (OwnerBiometricBindingTest's Device pattern) instead of the global singleton.
- When a PRD AC names a bug that has NO live implementation in this codebase (e.g. the "single prediction-error scalar" of SIGNAL-SPLIT), don't re-search for it — ground the story at the real layer where the analogous pre-collapse lives (here: `MemoryNode` storing only salience/accessCount + `MemoryImportanceScorer`'s composite), and RECORD the grounding in the story notes so the next reader stops re-grepping the false premise.
- `bash .ralph/progress.sh` is the honest progress readout (bar, per-story passes, live run line) and reads ONLY the live prd.json — never a hand-kept counter. It is called by ralph.sh at the start of a run, `--brief` per iteration, and at the end with `--note`. "Is a run in progress" is answered by the run's PID in `.ralph/.ralph_active` (`kill -0`), never by the marker's age, with a read-only /proc scan as fallback. Never name a network tool inside a file that some stored verifyCommand greps for network markers — the AC4-style clause `! grep -E 'curl '` bans the word even in a comment.
- Before writing ANY Kotlin import: check `.ralph/SYMBOL_FACTS.md` (auto-injected, permanent class->real-package memory, seeded from this repo's real `Unresolved reference` errors), then the file being edited, and only then grep the real tree — never guess a package by convention or from a neighbouring import. `.ralph/symbol_facts_check.sh [ClassName]` runs that whole loop (pick a real unrecorded class -> grep -> derive its package from the file's own `package` line -> record -> assert the real prompt, via `ralph.sh --print-prompt`, carries both the fact and the rule). `ralph.sh --print-prompt` prints the exact composed prompt with zero model calls — the cheap way to test what the model is told.
- ralph.sh runs a PREFLIGHT (real GitHub /user curl with $GITHUB_TOKEN, then a real one-token model probe) before ANY iteration, and exits 1 on failure with the raw output printed — use `--preflight-only` to test the model path without spending an iteration, `--skip-preflight` to bypass deliberately, and the four `PREFLIGHT_*` env knobs to shrink timeouts. To test ralph.sh's own control flow with ZERO real tokens, put a stub `opencode` (echo `ok`, log the last argv length) and a stub `curl` first on PATH: the probe call is ~31 chars vs ~49k for a real iteration call, so call counts prove the preflight is not an iteration; the stub curl MUST 401 an empty/`ghp_invalid*` bearer (otherwise ralph.sh re-verifying RALPH-FAST-PREFLIGHT recurses forever), wrap the run in `timeout -k 5 150`, and back up/restore progress.txt around it.
- `FakeModelBackend` is `final` — to prove WHICH model answered a generation, COMPOSE it as a `ModelBackend` decorator that logs `(handle.modelId, prompt, reply)` per `generate` (ReasoningTierServeGroundTruthTest.RecordingBackend pattern), never subclass. Per-tier model routing belongs on the generation REQUEST: `GenerateRequest.modelId` (default null → adapter-configured `currentModel`) lets `OllamaModelBackend.generate` pin `/api/chat` to `handle.modelId` so every consumer targets the handle's model automatically. `ModelManager.wake()` returns a CACHED on-demand handle WITHOUT re-consulting the governor — to force a DEGRADE_TO in one test, run `runCooldownSweep()` (after advancing the injected clock) to unload the cached reasoning handle first.
- A stored verifyCommand must be TIME-STABLE: ralph.sh re-runs every `passes:true` story's command on EVERY run start, so a command whose meaning depends on "the newest story diff" auto-rejects as soon as the next production story lands (bit AUDIT-TOOL after ONE-2). Pin fixture ranges for fixture-dependent proofs, or sweep the whole tree for repo-shape proofs (`vision_audit.sh --all` = AC1's "run cold against the current repo" shape); grep only content that is stable by construction (ONE-1's `Headline`+VISION.md greps survived; AUDIT-TOOL's ClaimKind-on-newest-diff greps did not). When a report is grepped as evidence, never let its header enumerate the things being counted for absence — `audit.py` caps `--paths` titles at 8 paths so a whole-tree report cannot NAME a file the detectors correctly left unflagged.

- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - Ephemeral-jarvis facts (Termux-relative paths, REPO_FACTS.md organ/CI bans for the OLD jarvis app) are stale for THIS "Friday" Stage-1 PRD: the app module is `:mobile:app`, gradlew is repo-root with `bash ./gradlew`, but the Stage-1 stories are PURE CI INFRASTRUCTURE work (.github/workflows + .ralph/ci_verify.sh) — the stored verifyCommands are static file/grep/bash checks, no gradle run, so no on-device build is involved despite REPO_FACTS's Termux-build ban.
  - prd.json gotcha (tripped twice THIS session): the `notes` field is the LAST field of each story object — after its closing `"` there must be a newline + `    },` with NO comma after the value (trailing comma = JSONDecodeError "Illegal trailing comma before end of object"; a MISSING closing quote instead = "Invalid control character"/"Expecting ',' delimiter"). Always `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('.ralph/prd.json'))"` after editing before committing.
  - `.ralph/ralph_run_*.log` is a LIVE transcript that the harness appends to, including echoing THIS session's tool calls — treat its contents as evidence of what prior runs did, never as a stable file.
- **Pre-existing failures observed (not in scope): NONE — this is a static-infra story; the stored verifyCommand (grep/bash, no gradle) exits 0 on the committed tree.

## Sat Sep 19 2026 - CI-VERIFY-BRIDGE
Thread: (local iteration)
- Closed the final passes:false story of the Stage-1 "Friday" PRD: added `.ralph/ci_verify.sh` (curl + jq + python3), the per-commit CI verification bridge for the GitHub Actions CI built by CI-BUILD-DEBUG-APK. Both stories now passes:true.
- Script behavior (AC1-AC6 all tested on this machine): owner/repo parsed from `git config --get remote.origin.url` (mhw77655-hue/Friday) with GITHUB_REPOSITORY fallback; HEAD sha via git rev-parse; API base https://api.github.com, token ONLY from $GITHUB_TOKEN (Authorization header, never a file; mktemp -d + EXIT trap); no run for HEAD -> `git push origin HEAD:main` then poll for run to appear -> wait-for-completion loop with 25-min cap (CIV_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=1500) and per-poll progress lines; downloads the 'junit-results' artifact; python3 verdict: exit 0 iff every TEST-*.xml testcase matching the --tests-style glob filter(s) (against classname and classname.method) passed AND >=1 matched, skips count as non-failing; exit 1 with failing FQCNs + failure-message lines otherwise; cancelled -> plain fail; 4-prereq fail-fast messages. --dry-run prints HEAD/repo/filter/token-set/plan with no network and exits 0 even tokenless. `bash -n` passes; no token constant in the script.
- Verdict logic proven standalone with synthetic XMLs: cognitive.* (1/3 failed -> exit 1 naming the failure), memory.* (1 matched all passed -> exit 0), model.* (no match -> exit 1), method-level filter -> exit 0. Real-mode fail-fast proven: tokenless real run prints 'GITHUB_TOKEN is not set...' exit 1.
- **CONTRADICTION discovered and repaired (documented in notes + REPO_FACTS):** the original story-2 stored verifyCommand ended with `! grep -rqE 'ghp_|github_pat_' .ralph/` (scan the whole .ralph dir for embedded token prefixes). That clause is SELF-REFERENTIALLY UNSATISFIABLE: the verifyCommand text itself is stored in .ralph/prd.json (and echoed into .ralph/ralph_run_*.log transcripts), so `grep -r .ralph/` ALWAYS matches its own pattern text and the story could never pass, no matter what ci_verify.sh contained. This is exactly the auto-reject-loop failure mode this stage exists to end. Repair (the ONLY change to the stored command): the scan now targets the .ralph TOOLING files where a token could be embedded -- `! grep -qE 'ghp_|github_pat_' .ralph/ci_verify.sh .ralph/ralph.sh .ralph/reject_story.py .ralph/prompt.md` -- matching AC6's script-scope intent; all four are clean (grep-verified on the committed tree). The old `grep -r .ralph/` would also scan prd.json, progress.txt, REPO_FACTS.md, and .ralph[1] run transcripts that legitimately mention the prefixes.
- Files changed/committed: .ralph/ci_verify.sh (new), .ralph/prd.json (story 2 passes:true + notes + repaired verifyCommand), .ralph/progress.txt (this entry), .ralph/REPO_FACTS.md (Stage-1 section extended).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - NEVER let a verifyCommand scan its OWN definition file: any `grep -r <dir>` where the verifyCommand text lives inside <dir> self-matches and the story auto-rejects forever. Scope token scans to the specific tooling files, or grammar-break the pattern (e.g. `[g]hp_`).
  - .ralph/prd.json `notes` values keep biting: write the FULL value including its closing `"` in a single edit (I dropped the trailing quote twice this run -> 'Invalid control character'), then always json.load() before commit.
  - Transcribe-vs-scan hygiene: .ralph/ralph_run_*.log + prompt.md are built from REPO_FACTS.md + transcripts, so anything a verifyCommand grep-scans must avoid the pattern literals even IN PROSE.
  - The GitHub REST run-lookup with `?head_sha=<sha>&per_page=30`, picking `.workflow_runs[]` by `endswith(build.yml)`, newest first, is the reliable way to find 'the CI run for HEAD' without the gh CLI (not installed on Termux).
- **Pre-existing failures observed (not in scope): NONE — pure .ralph tooling story; no gradle/Kotlin involved, both stored verifyCommands exit 0 on the committed tree.

commit 5613bc550cd3cb762d3453755f7b01f10da3d217
Author: root <root@localhost.localdomain>
Date:   Tue Oct 6 10:08:52 2026 +0100

    feat: ONE-2-COMPOSITION - One Friday: one composition root for the whole turn path, shared by the Android app and the JVM/Termux server, with injected time
    
    Repair main, which dc8a68f left red, and finish the story's evidence.
    
    Compilation:
    - AndroidPlatformPorts.reportFailure was an expression body inferring
      FailureEvent while PlatformPorts declares Unit; it is now a block body that
      reports and returns nothing.
    - TurnPathAssembly referenced FailureSurface without importing it.
    
    AC3 (one injected time source):
    - ModelManager.cooldown and ContinuityLaw.now now read the host's clock through
      the composition root, so the tier half-life and the SLOW rate limit move with
      one faked clock instead of a wall clock reached around the back of the root.
    - InjectedClockTest: a 72-hour jump on the host clock moves the spoken-ack gap,
      the on-demand tier cooldown and the SLOW layer's rate limit; each assertion
      fails on a wall clock. The slow path is not run there, so the test is about
      the clock rather than about the engine.
    - WallClockAllowlistTest: 68 declared (file, count) reads over a RECURSIVE walk
      of the turn-path packages, each with its own reason in a separate REASONS map
      (the previous version checked Int.isBlank() on a count and scanned nothing,
      because a directory scope entry was tested with isFile and skipped). Counts
      were re-derived offline against the same walk: 68 declared, 68 found, zero
      missing, zero changed. resource/ResourceGovernor.kt = 1 read (the admission
      record's last-activity stamp) joins the declared scope.
    
    Test-harness correctness:
    - TurnPathAssemblyTest: OrganWakeRequest.organRole is a String, so it takes
      OrganRole.REASONING.name.
    - Composition directories are unique dirs under java.io.tmpdir, never a JUnit
      TemporaryFolder: HumanCore.init is first-init-wins for the whole JVM, so the
      first dir handed the composition is that singleton's storage for the rest of
      the run, and a deleted folder is what broke OwnerBiometricBindingTest and
      PhaseBDisconnectedSubsystemWiringTest in run 36365413999.

# Ralph Progress Log
Started: Fri Aug 21 20:17:26 BST 2026

## Codebase Patterns
- For Kotlin-code stories in this GitHub-CI-backed repo, the stored verifyCommand = an optional static grep clause + `bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '<NewTestClass>.*'` class filters (exit 0 iff >=1 JUnit case matched and none failed in the CI junit-results artifact). The harness runs it after passes:true, so it pushes HEAD and polls the real CI run; filter mode ignores unrelated baseline failures. Local gradle stays banned.
- An auto-reject is only evidence about the CODE if the captured verify output (/tmp/ralph_verify_*.txt) shows a real test failure. `ci_verify.sh: GITHUB_TOKEN is not set` there is a HARNESS env failure — reproduce the true verdict by hand with the token set before debugging code. When `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` already equals HEAD and a completed run exists for that SHA, `bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '<filter>'` reuses it instantly (no push, no 25-min wait).
- Fixtures driven through the REAL CognitiveEngine path must avoid the `it`/`that`/`this` REFERENT-unknown substrings (even inside words like "favorite") and the VAGUE_ACTION verbs (do/make/handle/fix/take care of/deal with), or doubt hits 1.0 and the reasoning organ wakes unpredictably in otherwise-deterministic tests.
- TermuxJarvisServer IS the JVM-executable production composition root (mirrors JarvisEngine.init, discloses its JVM-safe doubles InMemoryGraph/DeterministicEmbedding in main source). For any 'exact production call path' AC proof of a JarvisEngine.init-wired seam, construct TermuxJarvisServer(port=0, backendOverride=FakeModelBackend()) and drive its engine through a recording LatencyPipeline with `bridgeSend = { captured.add(it) }` — the captured text IS the outgoing generation request payload (IdentityInChatPipelineGroundTruthTest, DialectIntegrationTest pattern). The only honest exception to 'swap only the 2 Android-bound stores': the last hop (modelManager.send) may be a recording collector, never an assertion on model reply text.
- CognitiveEngine nullable seams are added AFTER capabilityFabric and default null; each carries a KDoc 'Null keeps the pre-X path byte-for-byte'. Adding a seam = constructor param + read in processInput + (optionally) JarvisEngine.init + TermuxJarvisServer wiring + JarvisOrganGraph node+edge + SystemGraphTest pin.
- CONTINUITY-GATE-ENFORCED-SEAM (2026-09-14): per-turn generation signals now ride a single typed ContinuityGate (com.jarvis.app.continuity). All future per-turn payload signals should be added as new Snapshot fields inside ContinuityGate.snapshotForTurn, then surfaced through IdentityContext.gatherForTurn(snapshot) / ContextWindowAssembler.assembleFrom(snapshot) — the assembly hops accept the Snapshot ONLY, never raw signals.
- Custom Gradle `Test`/`JavaExec` tasks (verifyLiveOllama, runTermuxJarvisServer) must wire `classpath` from compile output DIRECTORY entries (`files(mainOut, testOut, runtimeCp)`); `files(...get().asFileTree)` expands to per-`.class`-file entries a JVM classloader cannot resolve (ClassNotFoundException). Task-level `filter.excludeTestsMatching` combines with command-line `--tests` (test runs iff matches an include AND no exclude), so class excludes reliably pull live tests out of wildcard sweeps. Kill processes by exact name (`pkill -x ollama`), never `pkill -f '<str>'`, when that string exists in the harness's own cmdline.
- prd.json `verifyCommand` fields are eval'd VERBATIM by ralph.sh (`eval "$VCMD"`). Never append result annotations, `->`, or parentheses to that field — any shell metacharacter becomes a syntax error and auto-rejects the story. Verification results go in `notes` / progress.txt only.
- Re-verifying an already-green full-sweep verifyCommand can silently return `testDebugUnitTest UP-TO-DATE` (cached-output reuse from a prior same-filter run) — that proves nothing. Force a genuine re-run by prefixing `:mobile:app:cleanTestDebugUnitTest` (deletes only test-result outputs; compiles stay up-to-date), then confirm the log shows the test task among the executed tasks and EXIT=0.
- JarvisEngine.init is the SINGLE production composition point: construct the real subsystems there and fill CognitiveEngine's nullable seams (blendedRetriever/graphStore/identityContext/capabilityFabric). It is an Android-bound `object` (Context/HandlerThread/Robolectric-unusable-on-aarch64) so can't be constructed in unit tests — prove wiring on the JVM by replicating init()'s composition in a harness that swaps ONLY the 2 Android-bound stores (FakeMemoryGraphStore, TestEmbeddingProvider) for JVM references, and drive the turn through the REAL LatencyPipeline -> CognitiveEngine.process. Every other object must be the real production class.
- PRODUCTION WIRING IS THIN: `JarvisEngine.init` passes only 5 of CognitiveEngine's 16 constructor params (scope/memoryStore/humanCore/modelManager/cognitiveAdmissionPolicy). A story marked `passes:true` proves the subsystem + tests are green, NOT that it is wired into the live turn. Before trusting a wiring claim, grep `JarvisEngine.kt` for the seam being filled; the only live advanced seam today is CognitiveAdmissionPolicy → ModelManager.wake.
- The one source-of-truth organ map (SystemGraph) is built ONLY inside SystemGraphTest.buildJarvisSystemGraph, is never instantiated in main, and is itself stale/incomplete (omits failure/body/humancore/voice/research/cloud/resolution/android; points safety.approvalMechanism at a non-existent class). Treat WiringDiagnostics output as the map's declaration, then cross-check with a production grep.
- Pre-existing-failure lists decay: the 6 CapabilityExecutorTest failures and memory.* native-download failures (long documented since BIBLE-STEP-1) are now RESOLVED on the current tree (only cognitive.planning GoalPlanner/CognitivePlanningIntegrationTest replan failures remain). Re-run before re-asserting any historical failure list.
- JUnit4 test methods must be void: `fun x() = runBlocking {...}` infers `Unit?` when the block tail is a safe call (`stored?.let{...}`) → InvalidTestClassError "should be void"; always write `runBlocking<Unit> {...}`.
- Kotlin override functions CANNOT declare default parameter values — the interface default only applies when the static type is the interface; call concrete typed overrides with all args.
- Construct singletons in `JarvisEngine.init` on the handler thread or main coroutine scope.
- `CognitiveEngine` takes `MemoryStorePort` (implemented by `MemoryStore`) and `HumanCore`.
- `MemoryStorePort` is a single-method interface (`queryMemories`) — test fakes only need that one method.
- Kotlin `@Volatile var property: Type?` automatically generates a `getProperty()` JVM getter, so defining explicit `fun getProperty()` causes a platform declaration clash.
- In coroutine Flow tests, collect events into a thread-safe list (`Collections.synchronizedList`) and await all expected events before cancelling the collector job.
- Set timeout >= 600000ms for initial Gradle Kotlin compilation on Termux/Android.
- When `EvolutionEngine` gates all candidates as REJECT, invoke `SelfRepairLoop` (if injected) for the diagnose/repair cycle.
- `SelfRepairLoop.repair()` takes `RepairRequirement`, a `suiteFilter` string, and a `verifier` lambda; use `""` for suiteFilter to match all Gradle XML results.
- Kotlin test backtick method names cannot contain `/` — use spaces instead.
- Gate 2 (SandboxExecutor) runs in `SelfRepairLoop.conclude()` BEFORE `PromotionGate.decide()` — the sandbox stress test tests the repaired `AlgorithmSpec` against adversarial inputs via `ResourceSandbox`, independently of Gradle verification results. `SelfRepairLoop` takes an optional `sandboxExecutor: SandboxExecutor?` constructor param; when null the sandbox gate is skipped (backward-compatible).
- `TopicTracker` uses Jaccard similarity over keyword sets with stopwords removed; boundary detection skips the turn immediately after a boundary fires to let the window accumulate turns in the new segment.
- Galaxy Memory tests use `FakeMemoryGraphStore` (in ConsolidationDaemonTest.kt) which stamps `validFrom` with real wall-clock time and whose `addFact` defaults salience to 0.5. Use `addFakeNode(MemoryNode(...salience=...))` to insert nodes with explicit salience/validFrom for decay tests.
- `MemoryImportanceScorer.decayedStrength` floors to `salienceFloor` for any node with `salience >= salienceFloor`. Under a short `baseHalfLifeMs` + large elapsed time, high AND low salience both floor to the same value (indistinguishable) — pick a baseHalfLifeMs large enough that the simulated elapsed time keeps both computed strengths above the floor.
- On this Termux device, `com.jarvis.app.memory.*` wildcard fails (EmbeddingProviderTest: 2 sqlite-vec .so download; MemoryGraphStoreTest: 7 xerial sqlite-jdbc native-lib). Scope verifyCommands to specific passing test classes.
- The ToolBuilder (Builder stack, §15) is constructed ONLY through `com.jarvis.app.builder.ToolBuilderComposition.build(...)` — one production composition point used by both JarvisEngine.init (via hosted requestBuild) and every test harness, so the stack cannot drift between production and tests. The builder must consume UniversalResearchEngine output through `researchPersist` (all wired channels → the store), carrying the received `MechanismCandidate`s on BuildResult.researchCandidates, never the local-only `research()` stub.
- A `ControlBackend` id MUST be a member of `DeviceControlRouter.ORDER` ("official-api"/"intent"/"media-session"/"accessibility"/"shizuku"/"adb") — the router sorts by `ORDER.indexOf(id)`, so any other id gets index -1 and is tried BEFORE every rung, silently destroying the documented fallback order the moment a second backend exists. ScreenBridgeControlBackend.id is "shizuku" (its true rung).
- New device-control backends: put ALL Android-runtime interaction behind an injectable port interface (e.g. `MediaSessionControlPort`) with a production `Platform...` impl that is a compile-time-only android.jar reference (device-only, like PlatformBiometricPromptResultMapper) — the backend then runs fully on the JVM against a deterministic fake port. `execute()` must return VERIFIED only when a re-read of platform state observably confirms the dispatch (never fire-and-assume).
- The process-global `HumanCore` singleton is mutated by any test that calls `HumanCore.init` + `OwnerBiometricBinder(HumanCoreOwnerBindingPort()).bind(...)` (PhaseBDisconnectedSubsystemWiringTest and OwnerBiometricBindingTest both do). Because JUnit class order is unstable, a later test asserting a FRESH `Bound` outcome can see `ReAuthenticated` and fail. New harnesses that only need "biometric authority signal from a real owner store" should bind through an isolated `HumanCoreGraph` over FileStorage (OwnerBiometricBindingTest's Device pattern) instead of the global singleton.
- When a PRD AC names a bug that has NO live implementation in this codebase (e.g. the "single prediction-error scalar" of SIGNAL-SPLIT), don't re-search for it — ground the story at the real layer where the analogous pre-collapse lives (here: `MemoryNode` storing only salience/accessCount + `MemoryImportanceScorer`'s composite), and RECORD the grounding in the story notes so the next reader stops re-grepping the false premise.
- `bash .ralph/progress.sh` is the honest progress readout (bar, per-story passes, live run line) and reads ONLY the live prd.json — never a hand-kept counter. It is called by ralph.sh at the start of a run, `--brief` per iteration, and at the end with `--note`. "Is a run in progress" is answered by the run's PID in `.ralph/.ralph_active` (`kill -0`), never by the marker's age, with a read-only /proc scan as fallback. Never name a network tool inside a file that some stored verifyCommand greps for network markers — the AC4-style clause `! grep -E 'curl '` bans the word even in a comment.
- Before writing ANY Kotlin import: check `.ralph/SYMBOL_FACTS.md` (auto-injected, permanent class->real-package memory, seeded from this repo's real `Unresolved reference` errors), then the file being edited, and only then grep the real tree — never guess a package by convention or from a neighbouring import. `.ralph/symbol_facts_check.sh [ClassName]` runs that whole loop (pick a real unrecorded class -> grep -> derive its package from the file's own `package` line -> record -> assert the real prompt, via `ralph.sh --print-prompt`, carries both the fact and the rule). `ralph.sh --print-prompt` prints the exact composed prompt with zero model calls — the cheap way to test what the model is told.
- ralph.sh runs a PREFLIGHT (real GitHub /user curl with $GITHUB_TOKEN, then a real one-token model probe) before ANY iteration, and exits 1 on failure with the raw output printed — use `--preflight-only` to test the model path without spending an iteration, `--skip-preflight` to bypass deliberately, and the four `PREFLIGHT_*` env knobs to shrink timeouts. To test ralph.sh's own control flow with ZERO real tokens, put a stub `opencode` (echo `ok`, log the last argv length) and a stub `curl` first on PATH: the probe call is ~31 chars vs ~49k for a real iteration call, so call counts prove the preflight is not an iteration; the stub curl MUST 401 an empty/`ghp_invalid*` bearer (otherwise ralph.sh re-verifying RALPH-FAST-PREFLIGHT recurses forever), wrap the run in `timeout -k 5 150`, and back up/restore progress.txt around it.
- `FakeModelBackend` is `final` — to prove WHICH model answered a generation, COMPOSE it as a `ModelBackend` decorator that logs `(handle.modelId, prompt, reply)` per `generate` (ReasoningTierServeGroundTruthTest.RecordingBackend pattern), never subclass. Per-tier model routing belongs on the generation REQUEST: `GenerateRequest.modelId` (default null → adapter-configured `currentModel`) lets `OllamaModelBackend.generate` pin `/api/chat` to `handle.modelId` so every consumer targets the handle's model automatically. `ModelManager.wake()` returns a CACHED on-demand handle WITHOUT re-consulting the governor — to force a DEGRADE_TO in one test, run `runCooldownSweep()` (after advancing the injected clock) to unload the cached reasoning handle first.
- A stored verifyCommand must be TIME-STABLE: ralph.sh re-runs every `passes:true` story's command on EVERY run start, so a command whose meaning depends on "the newest story diff" auto-rejects as soon as the next production story lands (bit AUDIT-TOOL after ONE-2). Pin fixture ranges for fixture-dependent proofs, or sweep the whole tree for repo-shape proofs (`vision_audit.sh --all` = AC1's "run cold against the current repo" shape); grep only content that is stable by construction (ONE-1's `Headline`+VISION.md greps survived; AUDIT-TOOL's ClaimKind-on-newest-diff greps did not). When a report is grepped as evidence, never let its header enumerate the things being counted for absence — `audit.py` caps `--paths` titles at 8 paths so a whole-tree report cannot NAME a file the detectors correctly left unflagged.

## Sat Sep 19 2026 - CI-VERIFY-BRIDGE
Thread: (local iteration)
- Closed the final passes:false story of the Stage-1 "Friday" PRD: added `.ralph/ci_verify.sh` (curl + jq + python3), the per-commit CI verification bridge for the GitHub Actions CI built by CI-BUILD-DEBUG-APK. Both stories now passes:true.
- Script behavior (AC1-AC6 all tested on this machine): owner/repo parsed from `git config --get remote.origin.url` (mhw77655-hue/Friday) with GITHUB_REPOSITORY fallback; HEAD sha via git rev-parse; API base https://api.github.com, token ONLY from $GITHUB_TOKEN (Authorization header, never a file; mktemp -d + EXIT trap); no run for HEAD -> `git push origin HEAD:main` then poll for run to appear -> wait-for-completion loop with 25-min cap (CIV_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=1500) and per-poll progress lines; downloads the 'junit-results' artifact; python3 verdict: exit 0 iff every TEST-*.xml testcase matching the --tests-style glob filter(s) (against classname and classname.method) passed AND >=1 matched, skips count as non-failing; exit 1 with failing FQCNs + failure-message lines otherwise; cancelled -> plain fail; 4-prereq fail-fast messages. --dry-run prints HEAD/repo/filter/token-set/plan with no network and exits 0 even tokenless. `bash -n` passes; no token constant in the script.
- Verdict logic proven standalone with synthetic XMLs: cognitive.* (1/3 failed -> exit 1 naming the failure), memory.* (1 matched all passed -> exit 0), model.* (no match -> exit 1), method-level filter -> exit 0. Real-mode fail-fast proven: tokenless real run prints 'GITHUB_TOKEN is not set...' exit 1.
- **CONTRADICTION discovered and repaired (documented in notes + REPO_FACTS):** the original story-2 stored verifyCommand ended with `! grep -rqE 'ghp_|github_pat_' .ralph/` (scan the whole .ralph dir for embedded token prefixes). That clause is SELF-REFERENTIALLY UNSATISFIABLE: the verifyCommand text itself is stored in .ralph/prd.json (and echoed into .ralph/ralph_run_*.log transcripts), so `grep -r .ralph/` ALWAYS matches its own pattern text and the story could never pass, no matter what ci_verify.sh contained. This is exactly the auto-reject-loop failure mode this stage exists to end. Repair (the ONLY change to the stored command): the scan now targets the .ralph TOOLING files where a token could be embedded -- `! grep -qE 'ghp_|github_pat_' .ralph/ci_verify.sh .ralph/ralph.sh .ralph/reject_story.py .ralph/prompt.md` -- matching AC6's script-scope intent; all four are clean (grep-verified on the committed tree). The old `grep -r .ralph/` would also scan prd.json, progress.txt, REPO_FACTS.md, and .ralph[1] run transcripts that legitimately mention the prefixes.
- Files changed/committed: .ralph/ci_verify.sh (new), .ralph/prd.json (story 2 passes:true + notes + repaired verifyCommand), .ralph/progress.txt (this entry), .ralph/REPO_FACTS.md (Stage-1 section extended).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - NEVER let a verifyCommand scan its OWN definition file: any `grep -r <dir>` where the verifyCommand text lives inside <dir> self-matches and the story auto-rejects forever. Scope token scans to the specific tooling files, or grammar-break the pattern (e.g. `[g]hp_`).
  - .ralph/prd.json `notes` values keep biting: write the FULL value including its closing `"` in a single edit (I dropped the trailing quote twice this run -> 'Invalid control character'), then always json.load() before commit.
  - Transcribe-vs-scan hygiene: .ralph/ralph_run_*.log + prompt.md are built from REPO_FACTS.md + transcripts, so anything a verifyCommand grep-scans must avoid the pattern literals even IN PROSE.
  - The GitHub REST run-lookup with `?head_sha=<sha>&per_page=30`, picking `.workflow_runs[]` by `endswith(build.yml)`, newest first, is the reliable way to find 'the CI run for HEAD' without the gh CLI (not installed on Termux).
- **Pre-existing failures observed (not in scope): NONE — pure .ralph tooling story; no gradle/Kotlin involved, both stored verifyCommands exit 0 on the committed tree.

    feat: ONE-2-COMPOSITION - One Friday: one composition root for the whole turn path, shared by the Android app and the JVM/Termux server, with injected time
    
    InjectedClockTest's own fixture takes only a clock (its resource snapshot is
    the fixed alwaysHealthy one), so the call site must stay `fixture.ports { now }`.
    Passing a snapshot as well was a compile error that CI run 37441513553 caught:
    the unit-test compile task failed while the job stayed green, because that step
    is continue-on-error and the junit-results artifact is what a filtered verify
    actually judges.

commit 5613bc550cd3cb762d3453755f7b01f10da3d217
Author: root <root@localhost.localdomain>
Date:   Tue Oct 6 10:08:52 2026 +0100

    feat: ONE-2-COMPOSITION - One Friday: one composition root for the whole turn path, shared by the Android app and the JVM/Termux server, with injected time
    
    Repair main, which dc8a68f left red, and finish the story's evidence.
    
    Compilation:
    - AndroidPlatformPorts.reportFailure was an expression body inferring
      FailureEvent while PlatformPorts declares Unit; it is now a block body that
      reports and returns nothing.
    - TurnPathAssembly referenced FailureSurface without importing it.
    
    AC3 (one injected time source):
    - ModelManager.cooldown and ContinuityLaw.now now read the host's clock through
      the composition root, so the tier half-life and the SLOW rate limit move with
      one faked clock instead of a wall clock reached around the back of the root.
    - InjectedClockTest: a 72-hour jump on the host clock moves the spoken-ack gap,
      the on-demand tier cooldown and the SLOW layer's rate limit; each assertion
      fails on a wall clock. The slow path is not run there, so the test is about
      the clock rather than about the engine.
    - WallClockAllowlistTest: 68 declared (file, count) reads over a RECURSIVE walk
      of the turn-path packages, each with its own reason in a separate REASONS map
      (the previous version checked Int.isBlank() on a count and scanned nothing,
      because a directory scope entry was tested with isFile and skipped). Counts
      were re-derived offline against the same walk: 68 declared, 68 found, zero
      missing, zero changed. resource/ResourceGovernor.kt = 1 read (the admission
      record's last-activity stamp) joins the declared scope.
    
    Test-harness correctness:
    - TurnPathAssemblyTest: OrganWakeRequest.organRole is a String, so it takes
      OrganRole.REASONING.name.
    - Composition directories are unique dirs under java.io.tmpdir, never a JUnit
      TemporaryFolder: HumanCore.init is first-init-wins for the whole JVM, so the
      first dir handed the composition is that singleton's storage for the rest of
      the run, and a deleted folder is what broke OwnerBiometricBindingTest and
      PhaseBDisconnectedSubsystemWiringTest in run 36365413999.

## Objective
- Implement the highest-priority pending story in `/sdcard/jarvis-repo/.ralph/prd.json`: **LAUNCH-SURVIVAL** (priority 0, `passes: false`) — make the installed debug APK survive launch on the Realme phone (fix two fatal crashes) and answer one typed turn through the resident model.
- Ralph agent workflow: PLAN first, implement one story per iteration, verify via CI only (`bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '<filter>'`), commit on main, flip `passes: true`, append to `.ralph/progress.txt`.

## Important Details
- Repo root: `/sdcard/jarvis-repo`; Android module `mobile/app/`; local gradle/assembleDebug is BANNED — GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/build.yml`) is the only build/test path; JDK 17.
- prd.json pending stories (all `passes:false`): LAUNCH-SURVIVAL (0), ONE-3-LIFETEST (10), ONE-4-RECORDER-LEDGER (11), ONE-5-IDENTITY-SYNC-FLOOR (12), ONE-6-SEED-REPORT (13); ONE-2-COMPOSITION was committed but reads `passes: false` (needs CI proof before flipping).
- LAUNCH-SURVIVAL crash facts (real phone, 2026-10-16): CRASH A (main proc, 1.7s): `IllegalArgumentException No enum constant com.jarvis.app.body.LanguageRouter.Language.EN` at `StreamingTts.synthesizeSegment(StreamingTts.kt:285)` on a Dispatchers.IO worker. CRASH B (:voice proc, 5s): `Ollama server does not register model 'resident'; registered models: jarvis-reasoning:latest,jarvis-resident:latest` at `OllamaModelBackend.loadModel(OllamaModelBackend.kt:58)`.
- Root causes identified:
  - CRASH A: `StreamingTts.kt:285` does `LanguageRouter.Language.valueOf(segment.language.uppercase())` but `TtsSegment.language` holds ISO/BCP-47 codes ("en"/"ar" from `LanguageSegment.language.code`) — enum constants are `ENGLISH`/`EGYPTIAN_ARABIC`, so `valueOf("EN")` always throws, even for valid tags.
  - CRASH B: `ModelManager.defaultModelId(tier)` (ModelManager.kt:287-291) falls back to literal `"resident"` when active provider's `config.modelId` is blank (active provider defaults to `heuristicAdapter`); `OllamaModelBackend.loadModel` does `require(modelId in registered)` → throws.
- Why main process warmed without CRASH B: `BodyCoordinator.warmModel` (BodyCoordinator.kt:649) calls `modelManager.send("")` → `attemptSend` → `generate` on `_activeProvider.value` (HeuristicAdapter default), never touching the registry/tiered `loadOnce` path; main died of CRASH A before its own anchor load could throw.
- `JarvisApplication.onCreate` calls `JarvisEngine.init(this)` unconditionally — Application.onCreate runs in EVERY process incl. `:voice` (AndroidManifest.xml:28 `android:process=":voice"` for `.voice.VoiceService`), so both processes build the whole graph. This is the AC5 root cause (one body vs second copy; VISION applies).
- AC requirements: AC1 failing repro tests first (per crash) with failing output pasted into notes; AC2 language tags 'EN','en','en-US','ar-EG','','xx-YY' never hit valueOf/closed enums, unknown → default voice + recorded as unknown; AC3 tier name resolution (exact → ":latest" form → degrade to registered model or explicit capability gap, never throws out of call; "resident" must resolve to `jarvis-resident:latest`); AC4 failing organ (speech synthesis loop, model load) must not kill app scope — failures recorded via existing `FailureSurface` with stack + plain-language line, next typed turn still served; AC5 one-body report per process in REPO_FACTS; AC6 incident file under `.ralph/incidents/` + INDEX.md + REPO_FACTS lines + full suite green.
- Untracked prior-iteration work exists: `mobile/app/src/test/java/com/jarvis/app/launchsurvival/FakeOllama.kt` (fake Ollama HTTP server: `/api/tags` returns jarvis-reasoning:latest + jarvis-resident:latest, `/api/chat`+`/api/generate` canned reply, `tagsStatus` knob) and `ResidentTierLoadTest.kt` (AC1(b): ModelManager.request(RESIDENT) → loadOnce → OllamaModelBackend over real HTTP must resolve `jarvis-resident:latest`; uses `OllamaAdapter(null).configure(ModelSource.Local(fake.host), ProviderConfig.Default)`). Also untracked: `mobile/app/src/test/java/com/jarvis/app/onefriday/EntryParityTest.kt` (ONE-2 work).
- Key architecture post-ONE-2: `com.jarvis.app.onefriday.TurnPathAssembly` is the single composition root (platform-free); `PlatformPorts` interface (imports no android.*) with `JvmPlatformPorts`/`AndroidPlatformPorts`; `stores.modelBackend()` builds the backend (JvmPlatformPorts ~L414: `return OllamaModelBackend(adapter)`); `reportFailure` seam exists on ports. `TermuxJarvisServer(port=0, backendOverride=FakeModelBackend(), ollamaHost=..., modelId=...)` is the JVM production composition twin.
- `FailureReport` (failure/FailureModel.kt:155): fields include subsystem, operation, severity, category, message, source, cause (stack), state, dependencies, failureCause, userMessage (plain line; else `UserFailureText.translate`); `FailureSurface.report()` never throws, dedupes into aggregates, exposes flows. `StreamingTts` already takes `onFailure: (FailureReport) -> Unit = {}`; JarvisEngine.kt:277 wires it to `failureSurface.report`.
- Test setup: `testImplementation junit:4.13.2` + `org.json:json:20240303` only; no Robolectric/mockito; `testOptions.unitTests.all` excludes the two live-Ollama classes. Android-bound classes (StreamingTts needs Context/TextToSpeech) can't be constructed in JVM tests → AC1(a)/AC4 speech-loop tests require extracting pure/seam code (e.g., a LanguageTagResolver / injectable synthesis-loop body) used by StreamingTts in production.
- `OllamaAdapter.listModels()` (adapters/OllamaAdapter.kt:142) GETs `/api/tags`, parses `models[].name`, throws IOException on non-success.
- Verify-command/transport rules: classify rejections by ci_verify.sh's own verdict line; `curl:` transport markers are not code verdicts; ci_verify.sh has 5-try download + 6×15s API retry; use `CIV_BRANCH=` for branch runs; a stored verifyCommand must be time-stable (pin filters or `--all`).
- prd.json `notes` is last field per story: no trailing comma, must keep closing quote; validate with `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('.ralph/prd.json'))"` after every edit.
- Fixtures on the real CognitiveEngine path must avoid `it`/`this`/`that` substrings and VAGUE_ACTION verbs (do/make/handle/fix/take care of/deal with) for low-doubt turns.

  - AC3: model-name resolution in `OllamaModelBackend.loadModel` (exact → `"$id:latest"` → registered name containing tier token e.g. `jarvis-resident:latest` → capability gap, never throw out of call for registry mismatch; transport IOException still throws but contained at the anchor call site); AC3 production-composition test: TurnPathAssembly/TermuxJarvisServer with modelBackend over FakeOllama, anchor resolves handle.modelId == "jarvis-resident:latest", typed turn returns generated text from FakeOllama `/api/chat` (open question: generation routes via `_activeProvider` (heuristic default) vs booked tier handle — need to determine how turn reaches FakeOllama; possibly switchProvider(OLLAMA, ModelSource.Local(fake.host)) or use tier-handle route).

I now have the complete picture. Here's where things stand.

## What we were doing
Implementing the top story in `.ralph/prd.json`: **LAUNCH-SURVIVAL** (priority 0, `passes:false`) — make the installed debug APK survive launch on the Realme by fixing two fatal crashes (CRASH A: bad enum `valueOf` in TTS; CRASH B: assumed model name `resident`) and answer one typed turn through the resident model.

## Findings this investigation confirmed (new, concrete)
- **CRASH A root cause pinned**: `StreamingTts.kt:285` — `LanguageRouter.Language.valueOf(segment.language.uppercase())`, but `segment.language` holds the 2-letter code `"en"` (set at `StreamingTts.kt:200` from `LanguageSegment.language.code`), while the enum constants are `ENGLISH`/`EGYPTIAN_ARABIC` (`LanguageRouter.kt:32-35`). `valueOf("EN")` always throws. The `TtsSegment.language` string is built *inside* `speak()`, not from untrusted input, but it is a raw tag → the resolution belongs in `synthesizeSegment`.
- **AC1(b) already written but untracked**: `mobile/app/src/test/java/com/jarvis/app/launchsurvival/{FakeOllama.kt, ResidentTierLoadTest.kt}` exist from the dead iteration and are exactly the AC1(b) repro (FakeOllama answers `/api/tags` with `jarvis-reasoning:latest`,`jarvis-resident:latest`; test drives `ModelManager.request(RESIDENT)` → `loadOnce` → `OllamaModelBackend` and asserts `jarvis-resident:latest`).
- **AC3 generation path is reachable deterministically**: `ModelManager.send()` → `attemptSend(tierHandle=booked)`; a tier handle booked via `noteTurnServe` makes `send()` call `modelBackend.generate(handle, …)` directly against the fake server. So an AC3 proof can drive the real `ModelManager` over `FakeOllama`, assert `loadModel`'s resolved `handle.modelId == "jarvis-resident:latest"`, and observe the generated text at the HTTP boundary. (`LatencyPipelineTest` runs a full composed turn with `engine=null` → `bridgeSend`; `send()` is async, so asserting `_lastReply` needs an await.)
- **Build test config**: `build.gradle.kts:99-106` excludes the two live-Ollama classes only; no `isReturnDefaultValues`; JUnit4 + `org.json` on the test classpath. Android-bound `StreamingTts`/`LanguageRouter` cannot be instantiated in JVM tests → the language-resolution logic must be extracted into a pure helper for AC1(a)/AC2/AC4.
- **AC5 cause confirmed**: `JarvisApplication.onCreate` → `JarvisEngine.init` (AndroidManifest `:voice`) and `LatencyLayer.installPipeline` all run in every process; `JarvisEngine.init` uses `scope = Dispatchers.Main` and does `scope.launch { anchorEngine.anchor() }` at `JarvisEngine.kt:489` — the anchor/model-load site needing AC4 containment and the source of the duplicate body.

## Blockers
- No CI possible right now: `/tmp/ralph_verify_1.txt` shows the harness preflight died at `GITHUB_TOKEN is unset` then HTTP 401 (bad/expired token). `verifyCommand` needs a working `$GITHUB_TOKEN`, which only you can supply.
- Working tree also carries untracked prior-iteration artifacts (`.ralph/audit/*` regenerated reports, `new_stories.json`, `prd_gate3b_done.json`, `EntryParityTest.kt`) and an uncommitted progress.txt tail — none committed.

Want me to (a) write out the explicit Phase-0 plan and start implementing LAUNCH-SURVIVAL now (failing AC1 tests first, then the production fixes), or (b) stop here until you provide a valid `GITHUB_TOKEN` so I can actually verify in CI?

package com.jarvis.app.model

import com.jarvis.app.model.adapters.OllamaAdapter
import java.io.IOException
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong

/**
 * OllamaModelBackend — the first REAL [ModelBackend] in the app.
 *
 * Ground truth (verified live, see .ralph/model_backend_proof.md): the local
 * Ollama server at 127.0.0.1:8080 is reachable and serves the imported local
 * GGUF model `jarvis-resident:latest` (LFM2.5-1.2B-Instruct-Q4_K_M). Every
 * operation here performs REAL HTTP against that server through the existing
 * [OllamaAdapter] — there is no fake success, no echo, no filesystem guess.
 *
 *  - [loadModel]: an Ollama model is "available" once the server has it
 *    registered. This probes the REAL registry (/api/tags) and refuses to
 *    register a handle for a model the server does not have — never a silent
 *    "answered success without a real model".
 *  - [generate]: REAL /api/chat inference through the existing adapter,
 *    returning the model's actual text.
 *  - [unloadModel]: Ollama serves models on demand; unloading invalidates the
 *    local handle only (mirrors OllamaAdapter.unload).
 *
 * This is not a second loader: it IS the single [ModelBackend] instance
 * ModelManager owns. No llama-server binary, no native library, no parallel
 * loading path.
 */
class OllamaModelBackend(
    private val ollama: OllamaAdapter
) : ModelBackend {

    private val loadedHandles = mutableSetOf<ModelHandle>()
    private val idSeq = AtomicLong(0)

    /** Successful [loadModel] calls — observable for tests, like FakeModelBackend. */
    @Volatile
    var loadCount: Int = 0
        private set

    /** Successful [generate] calls — observable for tests, like FakeModelBackend. */
    @Volatile
    var generateCount: Int = 0
        private set

    /** Snapshot of currently-loaded handles — observable for tests. */
    val loaded: Set<ModelHandle> get() = synchronized(loadedHandles) { loadedHandles.toSet() }

    override suspend fun loadModel(config: ModelBackendConfig): ModelHandle {
        val registered = try {
            ollama.listModels()
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            throw IOException(
                "Ollama server unreachable at 127.0.0.1:8080 — cannot load '${config.modelId}': ${e.message}"
            )
        }
        val modelId = config.modelId
        require(modelId in registered) {
            "Ollama server does not register model '$modelId'; registered models: ${registered.joinToString(",")}"
        }
        val handle = ModelHandle(id = idSeq.incrementAndGet(), modelId = modelId)
        synchronized(loadedHandles) { loadedHandles.add(handle) }
        loadCount++
        return handle
    }

    override suspend fun generate(handle: ModelHandle, prompt: String): String {
        check(isLoaded(handle)) {
            "OllamaModelBackend.generate on an unloaded handle $handle — a handle is valid only after loadModel()"
        }
        val result = ollama.generate(
            GenerateRequest(
                messages = listOf(Message(role = MessageRole.USER, content = prompt)),
                maxTokens = 200,
                // REASONING-TIER-MODEL-GROUND-TRUTH-AND-WIRE: the /api/chat
                // request must target the handle's model — the ON_DEMAND_REASONING
                // handle carries jarvis-reasoning, so a tier-served generation is
                // genuinely answered by the distinct reasoning model, never by the
                // adapter-configured resident model.
                modelId = handle.modelId
            )
        )
        generateCount++
        check(result.finishReason != FinishReason.ERROR && result.content.isNotBlank()) {
            "Ollama generate failed for '${handle.modelId}': finishReason=${result.finishReason} content='${result.content}'"
        }
        return result.content
    }

    override suspend fun unloadModel(handle: ModelHandle) {
        synchronized(loadedHandles) { loadedHandles.remove(handle) }
    }

    override fun isLoaded(handle: ModelHandle): Boolean =
        synchronized(loadedHandles) { handle in loadedHandles }

class LanguageRouter(
    private val context: Context,
    private val scope: CoroutineScope = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO)
) {

    // Supported languages
    enum class Language(val code: String, val locale: Locale, val ttsLanguage: String) {
        ENGLISH("en", Locale.US, "en-US"),
        EGYPTIAN_ARABIC("ar", Locale("ar", "EG"), "ar-EG")
    }

    private val _currentLanguage = MutableStateFlow(Language.ENGLISH)
    val currentLanguage: StateFlow<Language> = _currentLanguage.asStateFlow()

    /** Detect language from text (script-based). Blank text keeps the current language. */
    fun detectLanguage(text: String): Language {
        val trimmed = text.trim()
        if (trimmed.isBlank()) return _currentLanguage.value
        return LanguageDetection.detectLanguage(trimmed)
    }

    /** Detect language segments for code-switching. */
    fun detectSegments(text: String): List<LanguageSegment> = LanguageDetection.detectSegments(text)

    /** Get TTS voice for a language (selects best available). */
    fun getTtsVoice(language: Language, tts: TextToSpeech): android.speech.tts.Voice? {
        val voices = try { tts.voices } catch (e: Exception) { return null }
        return voices?.filter { it.locale.language == language.locale.language }
            ?.maxByOrNull { it.quality }
    }

    /** Set current language explicitly. */
    fun setLanguage(language: Language) {
        _currentLanguage.value = language
    }

/** Pre-synthesize common phrases to cache. */
    fun preloadPhrases(phrases: List<String>, language: LanguageRouter.Language) {
        scope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
            for (phrase in phrases) {
                synthesizeToCache(phrase, language)
            }
        }
    }

    private fun startSynthesisLoop() {
        scope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
            for (segment in pendingSynthesis) {
                synthesizeSegment(segment)
            }
        }
    }

    private fun startPlaybackLoop() {
        scope.launch {
            // Playback is handled by TextToSpeech callbacks
        }
    }

    private fun synthesizeSegment(segment: TtsSegment) {
        val lang = LanguageRouter.Language.valueOf(segment.language.uppercase())
        val ssml = toSsml(segment.text)

        // Check cache first
        val cacheKey = "${segment.text.hashCode()}_${segment.language}"
        val cachedFile = synthesisCache[cacheKey]

        if (cachedFile != null && cachedFile.exists()) {
            playCached(cachedFile, segment.isFinal)
        } else {
            // Synthesize and play
            synthesizeAndPlay(ssml, lang, segment.isFinal, cacheKey)
        }
    }

    private fun synthesizeAndPlay(ssml: String, language: LanguageRouter.Language, isFinal: Boolean, cacheKey: String) {
        selectBestVoiceForLanguage(language)

        val id = "tts_${currentUtteranceId++}"
        val cacheFile = File(cacheDir, "$cacheKey.wav")

        // Use synthesizeToFile for caching
        val result = engine?.synthesizeToFile(ssml, null, cacheFile, id)
        if (result == TextToSpeech.SUCCESS) {
            synthesisCache[cacheKey] = cacheFile
            trimCache()
            // Play via MediaPlayer for better control
            playCached(cacheFile, isFinal)
        } else {
            // Fallback to direct speak
            val speakResult = engine?.speak(ssml, TextToSpeech.QUEUE_ADD, null, id)
            if (result == null || speakResult != TextToSpeech.SUCCESS) {
                onFailure(
                    com.jarvis.app.failure.FailureReport(
                        subsystem = "STREAMING_TTS",
                        operation = "synthesize",

// the single backend, gated by the resource governor.
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------

    private fun roleForProvider(type: ModelProviderType): OrganRole = when (type) {
        ModelProviderType.HEURISTIC, ModelProviderType.NONE, ModelProviderType.CLOUD -> OrganRole.RESIDENT
        else -> OrganRole.REASONING
    }

    /**
     * REASONING-TIER-MODEL-GROUND-TRUTH-AND-WIRE: the ON_DEMAND_REASONING tier
     * always wakes the DISTINCT [TIER_MODEL_ID_REASONING] model — never the
     * active provider's resident modelId. The active-provider override applies
     * only to the resident tier (JarvisEngine.init configures OllamaAdapter with
     * OLLAMA_RESIDENT_MODEL = "jarvis-resident:latest"). Loaded handles carry
     * this modelId, and tier-served generation (send through the booked
     * reasoning handle) routes /api/chat to it via GenerateRequest.modelId.
     */
    private fun defaultModelId(tier: ModelTier): String = when (tier) {
        ModelTier.ON_DEMAND_REASONING -> TIER_MODEL_ID_REASONING
        else ->
            _activeProvider.value.config?.modelId?.takeIf { it.isNotBlank() } ?: when (tier) {
                ModelTier.RESIDENT -> "resident"
                ModelTier.VOICE -> "voice"
                ModelTier.VISION -> "vision"
                ModelTier.ON_DEMAND_REASONING -> TIER_MODEL_ID_REASONING
            }
    }

    /**
     * Wake an organ of this model in [role]'s tier and return its loaded
     * handle. This is the ONE loading path in the app — the tiered
     * [wake] result, reduced to the handle.
     */
    suspend fun request(
        role: OrganRole,
        task: String,
        modelId: String? = null,
        providerType: ModelProviderType? = null
    ): ModelHandle = wake(role, task, modelId, providerType).handle

    /**
     * Wake an organ and report which tier actually served it. The decision
     * ladder: an already-cached handle is returned directly; otherwise the
     * [ResourceGovernor] admits the wake ([AdmissionDecision.ALLOW] loads at
     * the requested tier, [AdmissionDecision.DENY]/[AdmissionDecision.DEGRADE_TO]
     * fall down the ladder and retry), and when the serve lands below the
     * requested tier [WakeResult.degraded] is true — the caller serves from
     * [WakeResult.servedTier] instead of throwing or hanging. The resident
     * tier is always allowed.
     */
    suspend fun wake(
        role: OrganRole,
        task: String,
        modelId: String? = null,
        providerType: ModelProviderType? = null
    ): WakeResult {
        var tier = role.tier
        while (true) {
            val cached = synchronized(lifecycleLock) { cachedByTier[tier] }
            if (cached != null) {
                synchronized(lifecycleLock) { lastUsedMs[tier] = clock() }
                return WakeResult(handle = cached, requestedTier = role.tier, servedTier = tier)
            }
            if (tier == ModelTier.RESIDENT) {
                val handle = loadOnce(tier, modelId = modelId ?: defaultModelId(tier), providerType = providerType)
                return WakeResult(handle = handle, requestedTier = role.tier, servedTier = tier)
            }
            when (val decision = resourceGovernor.admit(
                OrganWakeRequest(organRole = role.name, tier = tier, essential = true)
            )) {
                AdmissionDecision.ALLOW -> {
                    val handle = loadOnce(tier, modelId = modelId ?: defaultModelId(tier), providerType = providerType)
                    return WakeResult(handle = handle, requestedTier = role.tier, servedTier = tier)
                }
                AdmissionDecision.DENY ->
                    tier = tier.lowerTier
                        ?: throw IllegalStateException("No lower tier available for ${role.name}")
                is AdmissionDecision.DEGRADE_TO -> tier = decision.lowerTier
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Whether [tier] currently holds a loaded model handle (under the
     * lifecycle lock). The resident tier is the never-auto-unloaded anchor;
     * this read is what the AnchorEngine uses to verify the anchor is held
     * and to detect the moment a turn finds it missing.
     */
    fun isTierLoaded(tier: ModelTier): Boolean =
        synchronized(lifecycleLock) { cachedByTier.containsKey(tier) }
[0m
[0m$ [0mtail -c 8000 ralph_run_20261020_221714.log
ough the existing adapter,
 *    returning the model's actual text.
 *  - [unloadModel]: Ollama serves models on demand; unloading invalidates the
 *    local handle only (mirrors OllamaAdapter.unload).
 *
 * This is not a second loader: it IS the single [ModelBackend] instance
 * ModelManager owns. No llama-server binary, no native library, no parallel
 * loading path.
 */
class OllamaModelBackend(
    private val ollama: OllamaAdapter
) : ModelBackend {

    private val loadedHandles = mutableSetOf<ModelHandle>()
    private val idSeq = AtomicLong(0)

    /** Successful [loadModel] calls — observable for tests, like FakeModelBackend. */
    @Volatile
    var loadCount: Int = 0
        private set

    /** Successful [generate] calls — observable for tests, like FakeModelBackend. */
    @Volatile
    var generateCount: Int = 0
        private set

    /** Snapshot of currently-loaded handles — observable for tests. */
    val loaded: Set<ModelHandle> get() = synchronized(loadedHandles) { loadedHandles.toSet() }

    override suspend fun loadModel(config: ModelBackendConfig): ModelHandle {
        val registered = try {
            ollama.listModels()
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            throw IOException(
                "Ollama server unreachable at 127.0.0.1:8080 — cannot load '${config.modelId}': ${e.message}"
            )
        }
        val modelId = config.modelId
        require(modelId in registered) {
            "Ollama server does not register model '$modelId'; registered models: ${registered.joinToString(",")}"
        }
        val handle = ModelHandle(id = idSeq.incrementAndGet(), modelId = modelId)
        synchronized(loadedHandles) { loadedHandles.add(handle) }
        loadCount++
        return handle
    }

    override suspend fun generate(handle: ModelHandle, prompt: String): String {
        check(isLoaded(handle)) {
            "OllamaModelBackend.generate on an unloaded handle $handle — a handle is valid only after loadModel()"
        }
        val result = ollama.generate(
            GenerateRequest(
                messages = listOf(Message(role = MessageRole.USER, content = prompt)),
                maxTokens = 200,
                // REASONING-TIER-MODEL-GROUND-TRUTH-AND-WIRE: the /api/chat
                // request must target the handle's model — the ON_DEMAND_REASONING
                // handle carries jarvis-reasoning, so a tier-served generation is
                // genuinely answered by the distinct reasoning model, never by the
                // adapter-configured resident model.
                modelId = handle.modelId
            )
        )
        generateCount++
        check(result.finishReason != FinishReason.ERROR && result.content.isNotBlank()) {
            "Ollama generate failed for '${handle.modelId}': finishReason=${result.finishReason} content='${result.content}'"
        }
        return result.content
    }

    override suspend fun unloadModel(handle: ModelHandle) {
        synchronized(loadedHandles) { loadedHandles.remove(handle) }
    }

    override fun isLoaded(handle: ModelHandle): Boolean =
        synchronized(loadedHandles) { handle in loadedHandles }
}

# Ralph Progress Log
Started: Fri Aug 21 20:17:26 BST 2026
