## Codebase Patterns
- For Kotlin-code stories in this GitHub-CI-backed repo, the stored verifyCommand = an optional static grep clause + `bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '<NewTestClass>.*'` class filters (exit 0 iff >=1 JUnit case matched and none failed in the CI junit-results artifact). The harness runs it after passes:true, so it pushes HEAD and polls the real CI run; filter mode ignores unrelated baseline failures. Local gradle stays banned.
- An auto-reject is only evidence about the CODE if the captured verify output (/tmp/ralph_verify_*.txt) shows a real test failure. `ci_verify.sh: GITHUB_TOKEN is not set` there is a HARNESS env failure — reproduce the true verdict by hand with the token set before debugging code. When `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` already equals HEAD and a completed run exists for that SHA, `bash .ralph/ci_verify.sh '<filter>'` reuses it instantly (no push, no 25-min wait).
- Fixtures driven through the REAL CognitiveEngine path must avoid the `it`/`that`/`this` REFERENT-unknown substrings (even inside words like "favorite") and the VAGUE_ACTION verbs (do/make/handle/fix/take care of/deal with), or doubt hits 1.0 and the reasoning organ wakes unpredictably in otherwise-deterministic tests.
- TermuxJarvisServer IS the JVM-executable production composition root (mirrors JarvisEngine.init, discloses its JVM-safe doubles InMemoryGraph/DeterministicEmbedding in main source). For any 'exact production call path' AC proof of a JarvisEngine.init-wired seam, construct TermuxJarvisServer(port=0, backendOverride=FakeModelBackend()) and drive its engine through a recording LatencyPipeline with `bridgeSend = { captured.add(it) }` — the captured text IS the outgoing generation request payload (IdentityInChatPipelineGroundTruthTest, DialectIntegrationTest pattern). The only honest exception to 'swap only the 2 Android-bound stores': the last hop (modelManager.send) may be a recording collector, never an assertion on model reply text.
- CognitiveEngine nullable seams are added AFTER capabilityFabric and default null; each carries a KDoc 'Null keeps the pre-X path byte-for-byte'. Adding a seam = constructor param + read in processInput + (optionally) JarvisEngine.init + TermuxJarvisServer wiring + JarvisOrganGraph node+edge + SystemGraphTest pin.
- CONTINUITY-GATE-ENFORCED-SEAM (2026-09-14): per-turn generation signals now ride a single typed ContinuityGate (com.jarvis.app.continuity). All future per-turn payload signals should be added as new Snapshot fields inside ContinuityGate.snapshotForTurn, then surfaced through IdentityContext.gatherForTurn(snapshot) / ContextWindowAssembler.assembleFrom(snapshot) — the assembly hops accept the Snapshot ONLY, never raw signals.
- Custom Gradle `Test`/`JavaExec` tasks (verifyLiveOllama, runTermuxJarvisServer) must wire `classpath` from compile output DIRECTORY entries (`files(mainOut, testOut, runtimeCp)`); `files(...get().asFileTree)` expands to per-`.class`-file entries a JVM classloader cannot resolve (ClassNotFoundException). Task-level `filter.excludeTestsMatching` combines with command-line `--tests` (test runs iff matches an include AND no exclude), so class excludes reliably pull live tests out of wildcard sweeps. Kill processes by exact name (`pkill -x ollama`), never `pkill -f '<str>'`, when that string exists in the harness's own cmdline.
- prd.json `verifyCommand` fields are eval'd VERBATIM by ralph.sh (`eval "$VCMD"`). Never append result annotations, `->`, or parentheses to that field — any shell metacharacter becomes a syntax error and auto-rejects the story. Verification results go in `notes` / progress.txt only.
- Re-verifying an already-green full-sweep verifyCommand can silently return `testDebugUnitTest UP-TO-DATE` (cached-output reuse from a prior same-filter run) — that proves nothing. Force a genuine re-run by prefixing `:mobile:app:cleanTestDebugUnitTest` (deletes only test-result outputs; compiles stay up-to-date), then confirm the log shows the test task among the executed tasks and EXIT=0.
- JarvisEngine.init is the SINGLE production composition point: construct the real subsystems there and fill CognitiveEngine's nullable seams (blendedRetriever/graphStore/identityContext/capabilityFabric). It is an Android-bound `object` (Context/HandlerThread/Robolectric-unusable-on-aarch64) so can't be constructed in unit tests — prove wiring on the JVM by replicating init()'s composition in a harness that swaps ONLY the 2 Android-bound stores (FakeMemoryGraphStore, TestEmbeddingProvider) for JVM references, and drive the turn through the REAL LatencyPipeline -> CognitiveEngine.process. Every other object must be the real production class.
- PRODUCTION WIRING IS THIN: `JarvisEngine.init` passes only 5 of CognitiveEngine's 16 constructor params (scope/memoryStore/humanCore/modelManager/cognitiveAdmissionPolicy). A story marked `passes:true` proves the subsystem + tests are green, NOT that it is wired into the live turn. Before trusting a wiring claim, grep `JarvisEngine.kt` for the seam being filled; the only live advanced seam today is CognitiveAdmissionPolicy → ModelManager.wake.
- The one source-of-truth organ map (SystemGraph) is built ONLY inside SystemGraphTest.buildJarvisSystemGraph, is never instantiated in main, and is itself stale/incomplete (omits failure/body/humancore/voice/research/cloud/resolution/android; points safety.approvalMechanism at a non-existent class). Treat WiringDiagnostics output as the map's declaration, then cross-check with a production grep.
- Pre-existing-failure lists decay: the 6 CapabilityExecutorTest failures and memory.* native-download failures (long documented since BIBLE-STEP-1) are now RESOLVED on the current tree (only cognitive.planning GoalPlanner/CognitivePlanningIntegrationTest replan failures remain). Re-run before re-asserting any historical failure list.
- JUnit4 test methods must be void: `fun x() = runBlocking {...}` infers `Unit?` when the block tail is a safe call (`stored?.let{...}`) → InvalidTestClassError "should be void"; always write `runBlocking<Unit> {...}`.
- Kotlin override functions CANNOT declare default parameter values — the interface default only applies when the static type is the interface; call concrete typed overrides with all args.
- Construct singletons in `JarvisEngine.init` on the handler thread or main coroutine scope.
- `CognitiveEngine` takes `MemoryStorePort` (implemented by `MemoryStore`) and `HumanCore`.
- `MemoryStorePort` is a single-method interface (`queryMemories`) — test fakes only need that one method.
- Kotlin `@Volatile var property: Type?` automatically generates a `getProperty()` JVM getter, so defining explicit `fun getProperty()` causes a platform declaration clash.
- In coroutine Flow tests, collect events into a thread-safe list (`Collections.synchronizedList`) and await all expected events before cancelling the collector job.
- Set timeout >= 600000ms for initial Gradle Kotlin compilation on Termux/Android.
- When `EvolutionEngine` gates all candidates as REJECT, invoke `SelfRepairLoop` (if injected) for the diagnose/repair cycle.
- `SelfRepairLoop.repair()` takes `RepairRequirement`, a `suiteFilter` string, and a `verifier` lambda; use `""` for suiteFilter to match all Gradle XML results.
- Kotlin test backtick method names cannot contain `/` — use spaces instead.
- Gate 2 (SandboxExecutor) runs in `SelfRepairLoop.conclude()` BEFORE `PromotionGate.decide()` — the sandbox stress test tests the repaired `AlgorithmSpec` against adversarial inputs via `ResourceSandbox`, independently of Gradle verification results. `SelfRepairLoop` takes an optional `sandboxExecutor: SandboxExecutor?` constructor param; when null the sandbox gate is skipped (backward-compatible).
- `TopicTracker` uses Jaccard similarity over keyword sets with stopwords removed; boundary detection skips the turn immediately after a boundary fires to let the window accumulate turns in the new segment.
- Galaxy Memory tests use `FakeMemoryGraphStore` (in ConsolidationDaemonTest.kt) which stamps `validFrom` with real wall-clock time and whose `addFact` defaults salience to 0.5. Use `addFakeNode(MemoryNode(...salience=...))` to insert nodes with explicit salience/validFrom for decay tests.
- `MemoryImportanceScorer.decayedStrength` floors to `salienceFloor` for any node with `salience >= salienceFloor`. Under a short `baseHalfLifeMs` + large elapsed time, high AND low salience both floor to the same value (indistinguishable) — pick a baseHalfLifeMs large enough that the simulated elapsed time keeps both computed strengths above the floor.
- On this Termux device, `com.jarvis.app.memory.*` wildcard fails (EmbeddingProviderTest: 2 sqlite-vec .so download; MemoryGraphStoreTest: 7 xerial sqlite-jdbc native-lib). Scope verifyCommands to specific passing test classes.
- The ToolBuilder (Builder stack, §15) is constructed ONLY through `com.jarvis.app.builder.ToolBuilderComposition.build(...)` — one production composition point used by both JarvisEngine.init (via hosted requestBuild) and every test harness, so the stack cannot drift between production and tests. The builder must consume UniversalResearchEngine output through `researchPersist` (all wired channels → the store), carrying the received `MechanismCandidate`s on BuildResult.researchCandidates, never the local-only `research()` stub.
- A `ControlBackend` id MUST be a member of `DeviceControlRouter.ORDER` ("official-api"/"intent"/"media-session"/"accessibility"/"shizuku"/"adb") — the router sorts by `ORDER.indexOf(id)`, so any other id gets index -1 and is tried BEFORE every rung, silently destroying the documented fallback order the moment a second backend exists. ScreenBridgeControlBackend.id is "shizuku" (its true rung).
- New device-control backends: put ALL Android-runtime interaction behind an injectable port interface (e.g. `MediaSessionControlPort`) with a production `Platform...` impl that is a compile-time-only android.jar reference (device-only, like PlatformBiometricPromptResultMapper) — the backend then runs fully on the JVM against a deterministic fake port. `execute()` must return VERIFIED only when a re-read of platform state observably confirms the dispatch (never fire-and-assume).
- The process-global `HumanCore` singleton is mutated by any test that calls `HumanCore.init` + `OwnerBiometricBinder(HumanCoreOwnerBindingPort()).bind(...)` (PhaseBDisconnectedSubsystemWiringTest and OwnerBiometricBindingTest both do). Because JUnit class order is unstable, a later test asserting a FRESH `Bound` outcome can see `ReAuthenticated` and fail. New harnesses that only need "biometric authority signal from a real owner store" should bind through an isolated `HumanCoreGraph` over FileStorage (OwnerBiometricBindingTest's Device pattern) instead of the global singleton.
- When a PRD AC names a bug that has NO live implementation in this codebase (e.g. the "single prediction-error scalar" of SIGNAL-SPLIT), don't re-search for it — ground the story at the real layer where the analogous pre-collapse lives (here: `MemoryNode` storing only salience/accessCount + `MemoryImportanceScorer`'s composite), and RECORD the grounding in the story notes so the next reader stops re-grepping the false premise.
- `bash .ralph/progress.sh` is the honest progress readout (bar, per-story passes, live run line) and reads ONLY the live prd.json — never a hand-kept counter. It is called by ralph.sh at the start of a run, `--brief` per iteration, and at the end with `--note`. "Is a run in progress" is answered by the run's PID in `.ralph/.ralph_active` (`kill -0`), never by the marker's age, with a read-only /proc scan as fallback. Never name a network tool inside a file that some stored verifyCommand greps for network markers — the AC4-style clause `! grep -E 'curl '` bans the word even in a comment.
- Before writing ANY Kotlin import: check `.ralph/SYMBOL_FACTS.md` (auto-injected, permanent class->real-package memory, seeded from this repo's real `Unresolved reference` errors), then the file being edited, and only then grep the real tree — never guess a package by convention or from a neighbouring import. `.ralph/symbol_facts_check.sh [ClassName]` runs that whole loop (pick a real unrecorded class -> grep -> derive its package from the file's own `package` line -> record -> assert the real prompt, via `ralph.sh --print-prompt`, carries both the fact and the rule). `ralph.sh --print-prompt` prints the exact composed prompt with zero model calls — the cheap way to test what the model is told.
- ralph.sh runs a PREFLIGHT (real GitHub /user curl with $GITHUB_TOKEN, then a real one-token model probe) before ANY iteration, and exits 1 on failure with the raw output printed — use `--preflight-only` to test the model path without spending an iteration, `--skip-preflight` to bypass deliberately, and the four `PREFLIGHT_*` env knobs to shrink timeouts. To test ralph.sh's own control flow with ZERO real tokens, put a stub `opencode` (echo `ok`, log the last argv length) and a stub `curl` first on PATH: the probe call is ~31 chars vs ~49k for a real iteration call, so call counts prove the preflight is not an iteration; the stub curl MUST 401 an empty/`ghp_invalid*` bearer (otherwise ralph.sh re-verifying RALPH-FAST-PREFLIGHT recurses forever), wrap the run in `timeout -k 5 150`, and back up/restore progress.txt around it.
- `FakeModelBackend` is `final` — to prove WHICH model answered a generation, COMPOSE it as a `ModelBackend` decorator that logs `(handle.modelId, prompt, reply)` per `generate` (ReasoningTierServeGroundTruthTest.RecordingBackend pattern), never subclass. Per-tier model routing belongs on the generation REQUEST: `GenerateRequest.modelId` (default null → adapter-configured `currentModel`) lets `OllamaModelBackend.generate` pin `/api/chat` to `handle.modelId` so every consumer targets the handle's model automatically. `ModelManager.wake()` returns a CACHED on-demand handle WITHOUT re-consulting the governor — to force a DEGRADE_TO in one test, run `runCooldownSweep()` (after advancing the injected clock) to unload the cached reasoning handle first.
- A stored verifyCommand must be TIME-STABLE: ralph.sh re-runs every `passes:true` story's command on EVERY run start, so a command whose meaning depends on "the newest story diff" auto-rejects as soon as the next production story lands (bit AUDIT-TOOL after ONE-2). Pin fixture ranges for fixture-dependent proofs, or sweep the whole tree for repo-shape proofs (`vision_audit.sh --all` = AC1's "run cold against the current repo" shape); grep only content that is stable by construction (ONE-1's `Headline`+VISION.md greps survived; AUDIT-TOOL's ClaimKind-on-newest-diff greps did not). When a report is grepped as evidence, never let its header enumerate the things being counted for absence — `audit.py` caps `--paths` titles at 8 paths so a whole-tree report cannot NAME a file the detectors correctly left unflagged.

## Sat Sep 19 2026 - CI-VERIFY-BRIDGE
Thread: (local iteration)
- Closed the final passes:false story of the Stage-1 "Friday" PRD: added `.ralph/ci_verify.sh` (curl + jq + python3), the per-commit CI verification bridge for the GitHub Actions CI built by CI-BUILD-DEBUG-APK. Both stories now passes:true.
- Script behavior (AC1-AC6 all tested on this machine): owner/repo parsed from `git config --get remote.origin.url` (mhw77655-hue/Friday) with GITHUB_REPOSITORY fallback; HEAD sha via git rev-parse; API base https://api.github.com, token ONLY from $GITHUB_TOKEN (Authorization header, never a file; mktemp -d + EXIT trap); no run for HEAD -> `git push origin HEAD:main` then poll for run to appear -> wait-for-completion loop with 25-min cap (CIV_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=1500) and per-poll progress lines; downloads the 'junit-results' artifact; python3 verdict: exit 0 iff every TEST-*.xml testcase matching the --tests-style glob filter(s) (against classname and classname.method) passed AND >=1 matched, skips count as non-failing; exit 1 with failing FQCNs + failure-message lines otherwise; cancelled -> plain fail; 4-prereq fail-fast messages. --dry-run prints HEAD/repo/filter/token-set/plan with no network and exits 0 even tokenless. `bash -n` passes; no token constant in the script.
- Verdict logic proven standalone with synthetic XMLs: cognitive.* (1/3 failed -> exit 1 naming the failure), memory.* (1 matched all passed -> exit 0), model.* (no match -> exit 1), method-level filter -> exit 0. Real-mode fail-fast proven: tokenless real run prints 'GITHUB_TOKEN is not set...' exit 1.
- **CONTRADICTION discovered and repaired (documented in notes + REPO_FACTS):** the original story-2 stored verifyCommand ended with `! grep -rqE 'ghp_|github_pat_' .ralph/` (scan the whole .ralph dir for embedded token prefixes). That clause is SELF-REFERENTIALLY UNSATISFIABLE: the verifyCommand text itself is stored in .ralph/prd.json (and echoed into .ralph/ralph_run_*.log transcripts), so `grep -r .ralph/` ALWAYS matches its own pattern text and the story could never pass, no matter what ci_verify.sh contained. This is exactly the auto-reject-loop failure mode this stage exists to end. Repair (the ONLY change to the stored command): the scan now targets the .ralph TOOLING files where a token could be embedded -- `! grep -qE 'ghp_|github_pat_' .ralph/ci_verify.sh .ralph/ralph.sh .ralph/reject_story.py .ralph/prompt.md` -- matching AC6's script-scope intent; all four are clean (grep-verified on the committed tree). The old `grep -r .ralph/` would also scan prd.json, progress.txt, REPO_FACTS.md, and .ralph[1] run transcripts that legitimately mention the prefixes.
- Files changed/committed: .ralph/ci_verify.sh (new), .ralph/prd.json (story 2 passes:true + notes + repaired verifyCommand), .ralph/progress.txt (this entry), .ralph/REPO_FACTS.md (Stage-1 section extended).
- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - NEVER let a verifyCommand scan its OWN definition file: any `grep -r <dir>` where the verifyCommand text lives inside <dir> self-matches and the story auto-rejects forever. Scope token scans to the specific tooling files, or grammar-break the pattern (e.g. `[g]hp_`).
  - .ralph/prd.json `notes` values keep biting: write the FULL value including its closing `"` in a single edit (I dropped the trailing quote twice this run -> 'Invalid control character'), then always json.load() before commit.
  - Transcribe-vs-scan hygiene: .ralph/ralph_run_*.log + prompt.md are built from REPO_FACTS.md + transcripts, so anything a verifyCommand grep-scans must avoid the pattern literals even IN PROSE.
  - The GitHub REST run-lookup with `?head_sha=<sha>&per_page=30`, picking `.workflow_runs[]` by `endswith(build.yml)`, newest first, is the reliable way to find 'the CI run for HEAD' without the gh CLI (not installed on Termux).
- **Pre-existing failures observed (not in scope): NONE — pure .ralph tooling story; no gradle/Kotlin involved, both stored verifyCommands exit 0 on the committed tree.

- **Learnings for future iterations:**
  - The passages are written as ONE unwrapped line each. That is deliberate: the story's own grep targets (`legacy scaffolding, quarantined`, `Two compositions exist`, `replay of the original episode text blocked`, `seed of the final FRIDAY`) are substrings that hard-wrapping would split across a line break and silently break the stored verifyCommand forever. Markdown renders identically. Do not re-wrap them.
  - Story passages in a prd.json `description` are delimited by `' ` (quote + SPACE), not by `' . ` - there is no sentence-ending period after the closing quote. Apostrophes INSIDE a passage (`Venon's`) also make naive quote-pair parsing wrong. And the passage order in the description is not the section order: P4 is followed by PASSAGE P6, only P8 is followed by PASSAGES B1-B3.
  - A non-greedy `'(.*?)'\s+NEXT` regex anchored on the wrong next-passage label does not fail loudly - it silently swallows the two following passages (and their instruction text) into one 2159-char "passage". The cheap guards that caught it: assert the extracted text contains no `PASSAGE`, assert it ends with `.`, and assert it appears in the destination file exactly once. Two of the three greedy passages then looked "duplicated" in the file, which is what exposed it.
  - `bash .ralph/audit_check.sh` is the cheap regression oracle for any VISION.md edit: it proves Sections 4 and 13 still parse and that Section 12 is still structurally unread, with no network and no gradle. Run it after touching the file, not just `vision_audit.sh`.
  - The audit report under `.ralph/audit/` is regenerated (timestamp only) by every `vision_audit.sh` run, including the harness's own re-verification of a stored verifyCommand - expect it to be dirty after verification and do not read that diff as a finding change.

### id
LAUNCH-SURVIVAL
### title
The installed build survives launch and answers one typed turn
### description
Real-phone evidence, 2026-10-16 23:20, debug APK installed on the Realme with Ollama serving at 127.0.0.1:8080 (registered models jarvis-resident:latest and jarvis-reasoning:latest): two fatal crashes inside 5 seconds. CRASH A, main process, 1.7 s after launch: IllegalArgumentException No enum constant com.jarvis.app.body.LanguageRouter.Language.EN at StreamingTts.synthesizeSegment(StreamingTts.kt:285); an uncaught exception on a Dispatchers.IO worker killed the whole app. CRASH B, :voice process, 5 s after launch: IllegalArgumentException Ollama server does not register model 'resident'; registered models: jarvis-reasoning:latest,jarvis-resident:latest at OllamaModelBackend.loadModel(OllamaModelBackend.kt:58). Ollama saw only GET /health (404) and GET /api/tags (200), never a generate call. Also seen, non-fatal: BodyCoordinator, PresenceMonitor and 'Model warmed' log in both processes (main pid 4039 and :voice pid 4175); ObsidianSync logTurn failed ENOENT for /storage/emulated/0/JarvisSync/vault/JARVIS/2026-10-16.md. Outcome wanted: on this phone, with this Ollama, she launches, stays up, and answers one typed turn through the resident model. Fix the causes, not the symptoms. VISION applies: no closed set of kinds fed by outside input, unknown values degrade instead of throwing, one body not a second copy.
### acceptanceCriteria
["AC1 Reproduce first. Before changing production code, add tests in package com.jarvis.app.launchsurvival that FAIL on the current code, one per crash, and paste the failing output into notes: (a) the speech path given the language tags 'EN', 'en', 'en-US', 'ar-EG', an empty string and 'xx-YY' must not throw and must end with a voice choice; (b) a fake Ollama HTTP server answering /api/tags exactly as the real one did (names jarvis-reasoning:latest and jarvis-resident:latest) behind the production model-loading path: loading the resident tier must resolve to jarvis-resident:latest.", 'AC2 Language is open. Outside language strings are never passed to valueOf or any closed enum anywhere on the speech path; an unknown, empty or differently-cased tag routes to the default voice and is recorded as unknown. The audit tool reports zero new findings for this story or lists them in notes.', "AC3 Names are resolved, not assumed. A tier name resolves against what the server actually registers (exact name, then the ':latest' form, then a degrade to a registered model or an explicit capability gap). A registry that lacks the tier degrades or reports a gap and never throws out of the call. Through the production composition, against the fake server from AC1, a typed turn returns generated text. Notes state from the code where 'resident' came from and why the main process warmed without this error.", 'AC4 A failing organ does not kill the body. Tests make the speech synthesis loop and the model load throw; the app-level scope keeps serving the next typed turn, and each failure is recorded through the existing FailureSurface with its stack and one plain-language line. Nothing is swallowed silently.', 'AC5 One body, report only. From the code, with file:line, state in notes which components each process constructs (main and :voice) and why BodyCoordinator, PresenceMonitor and the model warm run in both. I now have the complete picture. Here's where things stand.

## What we were doing
Implementing the top story in `.ralph/prd.json`: **LAUNCH-SURVIVAL** (priority 0, `passes:false`) — make the installed debug APK survive launch on the Realme by fixing two fatal crashes (CRASH A: bad enum `valueOf` in TTS; CRASH B: assumed model name `resident`) and answer one typed turn through the resident model.

## Findings this investigation confirmed (new, concrete)
- **CRASH A root cause pinned**: `StreamingTts.kt:285` — `LanguageRouter.Language.valueOf(segment.language.uppercase())`, but `segment.language` holds the 2-letter code `"en"` (set at `StreamingTts.kt:200` from `LanguageSegment.language.code`), while the enum constants are `ENGLISH`/`EGYPTIAN_ARABIC` (`LanguageRouter.kt:32-35`). `valueOf("EN")` always throws. The `TtsSegment.language` string is built *inside* `speak()`, not from untrusted input, but it is a raw tag → the resolution belongs in `synthesizeSegment`.
- **AC1(b) already written but untracked**: `mobile/app/src/test/java/com/jarvis/app/launchsurvival/{FakeOllama.kt, ResidentTierLoadTest.kt}` exist from the dead iteration and are exactly the AC1(b) repro (FakeOllama answers `/api/tags` with `jarvis-reasoning:latest`,`jarvis-resident:latest`; test drives `ModelManager.request(RESIDENT)` → `loadOnce` → `OllamaModelBackend` and asserts `jarvis-resident:latest`).
- **AC3 generation path is reachable deterministically**: `ModelManager.send()` → `attemptSend(tierHandle=booked)`; a tier handle booked via `noteTurnServe` makes `send()` call `modelBackend.generate(handle, …)` directly against the fake server. So an AC3 proof can drive the real `ModelManager` over `FakeOllama`, assert `loadModel`'s resolved `handle.modelId == "jarvis-resident:latest"`, and observe the generated text at the HTTP boundary. (`LatencyPipelineTest` runs a full composed turn with `engine=null` → `bridgeSend`; `send()` is async, so asserting `_lastReply` needs an await.)
- **Build test config**: `build.gradle.kts:99-106` excludes the two live-Ollama classes only; no `isReturnDefaultValues`; JUnit4 + `org.json` on the test classpath. Android-bound `StreamingTts`/`LanguageRouter` cannot be instantiated in JVM tests → the language-resolution logic must be extracted into a pure helper for AC1(a)/AC2/AC4.
- **AC5 cause confirmed**: `JarvisApplication.onCreate` → `JarvisEngine.init` (AndroidManifest `:voice`) and `LatencyLayer.installPipeline` all run in every process; `JarvisEngine.init` uses `scope = Dispatchers.Main` and does `scope.launch { anchorEngine.anchor() }` at `JarvisEngine.kt:489` — the anchor/model-load site needing AC4 containment and the source of the duplicate body.

## Blockers
- No CI possible right now: `/tmp/ralph_verify_1.txt` shows the harness preflight died at `GITHUB_TOKEN is unset` then HTTP 401 (bad/expired token). `verifyCommand` needs a working `$GITHUB_TOKEN`, which only you can supply.
- Working tree also carries untracked prior-iteration artifacts (`.ralph/audit/*` regenerated reports, `new_stories.json`, `prd_gate3b_done.json`, `EntryParityTest.kt`) and an uncommitted progress.txt tail — none committed.

Want me to (a) write out the explicit Phase-0 plan and start implementing LAUNCH-SURVIVAL now (failing AC1 tests first, then the production fixes), or (b) stop here until you provide a valid `GITHUB_TOKEN` so I can actually verify in CI?

/**
 * ONE-2-COMPOSITION, AC1 and AC5: ONE composition root, exercised through the
 * two entry points and two different adapters.
 *
 * AC1 asks that "both entry points obtain their whole object graph from the
 * same composition root and differ only through the platform adapter", with a
 * test that fails if the constructed component set differs by anything other
 * than adapter-provided parts.
 *
 * The JVM cannot run the Android runtime, so the Android HALF of that is proven
 * structurally (below, test 1): the Android entry point, like the JVM entry
 * point, contains exactly one call to `TurnPathAssembly.assemble(...)` and
 * constructs none of the organs itself, so its graph is the root's graph by
 * construction. The functional HALF (tests 2) builds two real compositions —
 * the production JVM host and an independently configured test adapter — and
 * compares the component SET of both assemblies field by field; anything that
 * differs must be on the documented adapter-provided list.
 *
 * AC5 asks for two adapter implementations (Android + JVM-test); test 3 pins
 * both classes against the shared [PlatformPorts] seam.
 */
class EntryParityTest {

    // ── AC1: the graph comes from the one root, on both entry points ─────────

    @Test
    fun `AC1 both entry points obtain the whole object graph from the one composition root`() {
        val root = sourceRoot()

        listOf(
            "JarvisEngine.kt" to "the Android entry point",
            "termux/TermuxJarvisServer.kt" to "the JVM/Termux entry point"
        ).forEach { (relative, label) ->
            val src = File(root, relative).readText()
            assertEquals(
                "$label must obtain its graph once, from TurnPathAssembly.assemble",
                1,
                Regex("""TurnPathAssembly\.assemble\(""").findAll(src).count()
===

// ships the same path — the native merge rejects the duplicate. sherpa's
    // copy is the canonical one for the shipped ABI; pickFirst resolves it.
    // Without this the APK cannot assemble, blocking on-device verification.
    //
    // PRE-EXISTING BUILD FIX (2/2): this host is an aarch64 container, but the
    // NDK ships an x86_64 llvm-strip that cannot execute here, so AGP's
    // stripDebugSymbols step fails on any APK containing native libs. Keeping
    // debug symbols in all .so skips the strip step entirely. Debug-only

val runTermuxJarvisServer by tasks.registering(JavaExec::class) {
    group = "application"
    description = "Run the JARVIS Termux live instance (localhost browser interface)."
    val mainOut = tasks.named("compileDebugKotlin", KotlinCompile::class).get().destinationDirectory

    // Pass-through: --port=8081 --ollama=127.0.0.1:8080 --model=<id>

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// RALPH-VERIFY-DECOUPLE-LIVE-OLLAMA companion task: `testOptions.unitTests.all`
// (inside the android{} block above) EXCLUDES the two live-Ollama classes from
// every default sweep; this task runs those exact two classes on demand.
val verifyLiveOllama = tasks.register<Test>("verifyLiveOllama") {
    group = "verification"
    description = "Runs ONLY the two on-device live-Ollama test classes (OllamaModelBackendTest + OllamaModelBackendLiveWiringTest) against the local server at 127.0.0.1:8080. The default unit-test tasks exclude them; run THIS task on demand (server must be warm)."
    dependsOn("compileDebugKotlin", "compileDebugUnitTestKotlin")
    val mainOut = tasks.named("compileDebugKotlin", KotlinCompile::class).get().destinationDirectory
    val testOut = tasks.named("compileDebugUnitTestKotlin", KotlinCompile::class).get().destinationDirectory
    val runtimeCp = configurations.named("debugUnitTestRuntimeClasspath").get()
    // Classpath entries must be the output DIRECTORIES, not per-file expansions:
    // a JVM classloader cannot resolve package structure from individual
    // `.class` file entries (expanding with .asFileTree yields exactly that and
    // produces ClassNotFoundException for every test class).
    testClassesDirs = files(testOut)
    classpath = files(mainOut, testOut, runtimeCp)
    filter {
        includeTestsMatching("com.jarvis.app.latency.OllamaModelBackendLiveWiringTest")
        includeTestsMatching("com.jarvis.app.model.OllamaModelBackendTest")
        isFailOnNoMatchingTests = true
    }
}

[0m$ [0msed -n '1,120p' /sdcard/jarvis-repo/.ralph/vision_audit.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# vision_audit.sh -- run the VISION.md Section 13 audit of what a story built,
# and print the report.
#
# Usage:
#   vision_audit.sh                     # the diff the newest story produced
#   vision_audit.sh --all               # every production Kotlin file, cold
#   vision_audit.sh --base <ref> --head <ref>
#   vision_audit.sh --paths <file>...   # whole files instead of a diff
#   vision_audit.sh --self-audit        # audit this tool's own source
#   vision_audit.sh --make-branch audit/<name> [--run-ci audit/<name>]
#   vision_audit.sh --help
#
# WHAT THE DEFAULT IS, AND WHY IT IS NOT A HINT
# --------------------------------------------
# The audit is of the diff a story produced, so the default range is the newest
# RUN of consecutive commits that changed production Kotlin under
# mobile/app/src/main/java: the feature commit plus the fixes that follow it,
# up to the first parent that did not touch it. A docs-only commit does not
# move that range, and neither does a fix inside it get dropped, because a
# fix is part of the story's real diff. The range is derived from `git log` at
# run time; nothing in this repo tells it which commit or which findings to
# expect, and it never reads VISION.md Section 12, where the known deviations
# are disclosed by name (audit.py extracts Sections 4 and 13 only, and
# audit_check.sh proves it).
#
# Pass --all to sweep the whole production tree instead, which is the AC1 "run
# cold against the current repo" shape and is much noisier: every closed type
# and every constant in the module is reported, because on a whole-repo pass
# none of them was "added by a story".
#
# OUTPUT
# ------
# stdout: the full report, and nothing else, so it can be piped or grepped as
#         the product rather than as a log.
# stderr: the one-line-per-finding index audit.py prints, so a human watching
#         a terminal still sees the summary.
#
# WHAT IT IS NOT
# --------------
# It reports; it never enforces. It does not edit source, does not touch
# prd.json, and has no path that merges a branch or closes a story. The patch
# it can produce is a diff on its own `audit/**` branch, and --run-ci pushes
# that branch and reads CI's verdict. Merging remains a human action.
#
# COST
# ----
# Five of the six checks are parse-level and spend nothing. Only the
# ownership-naming check may call a model, at most once per run, and only when
# AUDIT_MODEL_CMD names a command that reads a prompt on stdin. With no such
# command the report says the names are UNJUDGED rather than guessing.
#
# On the executable bit: this repository is checked out on a filesystem that
# does not keep it (chmod +x is silently dropped and core.fileMode is false), so
# every script here is invoked as `bash .ralph/<name>.sh` and no stored check may
# require `test -x`.
set -uo pipefail

HERE="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
REPO="$(cd -- "$HERE/.." && pwd)"
cd "$REPO" || exit 2

SOURCE_PREFIX="mobile/app/src/main/java"

if [ ! -f "$HERE/audit.py" ]; then
  echo "vision_audit.sh: $HERE/audit.py is missing; there is nothing to run." >&2
  exit 2
fi

# No arguments at all: choose the range. With any argument, pass them through
# untouched so audit.py's own flags (and --help) are reachable.
if [ "$#" -eq 0 ]; then
  newest="$(git log -n 1 --format=%H -- "$SOURCE_PREFIX" 2>/dev/null || true)"
  if [ -z "$newest" ]; then
    echo "vision_audit.sh: no commit under $SOURCE_PREFIX yet; there is no story" \
         "diff to audit. Pass --all to sweep the tree instead." >&2
    exit 2
  fi
  # Walk the run back while each parent also touched production Kotlin.
  first="$newest"
  while :; do
    parent="$(git rev-parse --verify --quiet "${first}^" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    [ -n "$parent" ] || break
    [ "$(git log -n 1 --format=%H "$parent" -- "$SOURCE_PREFIX" 2>/dev/null || true)" \
      = "$parent" ] || break
    first="$parent"
  done
  base="$(git rev-parse --verify --quiet "${first}^" 2>/dev/null || true)"
  if [ -z "$base" ]; then
    # The very first commit in the repo: diff against the empty tree, which is
    # what that commit's parent would have been.
    base="$(git hash-object -t tree /dev/null)"
  fi
  echo "vision_audit.sh: auditing the newest production-Kotlin diff ${base:0:10}..${newest:0:10} (${first:0:10} is where that run of story commits starts)" >&2
  set -- --base "$base" --head "$newest"
elif [ "$1" = "--all" ]; then
  # Every tracked production Kotlin file. `--` pathspec-safe, and the empty case
  # is reported by audit.py rather than turned into an empty audit here.
  shift
  echo "vision_audit.sh: auditing every tracked file under $SOURCE_PREFIX" >&2
  set -- --paths $(git ls-files -- "$SOURCE_PREFIX") "$@"
fi

summary="$(mktemp)"
trap 'rm -f "$summary"' EXIT

# audit.py writes the report, the patch when it has one, and a JSON sidecar into
# .ralph/audit/, and prints its own index. Its index goes to stderr; the report
# it names is the stdout of this script.
python3 "$HERE/audit.py" "$@" >"$summary"
status=$?
cat "$summary" >&2

report="$(sed -n 's/^  report   : //p' "$summary" | tail -1)"
if [ -z "$report" ] || [ ! -f "$report" ]; then
  echo "vision_audit.sh: audit.py produced no report (exit ${status}); its own" \
       "message above is the whole answer." >&2
  exit "${status:-2}"
fi

Now I have complete understanding. Let me write the Phase 0 plan.

## PHASE 0 PLAN — LAUNCH-SURVIVAL

**Story:** Two fatal launch crashes on the phone (CRASH A: bad enum `valueOf` in TTS; CRASH B: assumed model name `resident`). Outcome: phone launches, stays up, answers one typed turn through the resident model.

**Root causes (verified by reading real source, not assumed):**

- **CRASH A** — `StreamingTts.kt:285`: `LanguageRouter.Language.valueOf(segment.language.uppercase())`. `TtsSegment.language` is a raw tag (`"en"`/`"ar"` from `LanguageSegment.language.code`, set at `StreamingTts.kt:200`), but the enum constants are `ENGLISH`/`EGYPTIAN_ARABIC`. `valueOf("EN")` throws `IllegalArgumentException`, and the synthesis loop runs on a `Dispatchers.IO` worker with no catch — the uncaught exception kills the process. Fix: an open resolver (`LanguageTagResolver`) that never calls `valueOf` on outside text; unknown/empty/odd-cased tags degrade to a default voice and are recorded as unknown.
- **CRASH B** — `ModelManager.defaultModelId(ModelTier.RESIDENT)` returns the literal `"resident"` when the active provider's config has no modelId. The phone's env switch left `ProviderConfig.Default`, so `OllamaModelBackend.loadModel` requires `"resident" in registered` against a server registering `jarvis-resident:latest`/`jarvis-reasoning:latest` → throws. Fix: resolve a tier name against what the server actually registers (exact → `:latest` → degrade to a registered model or an explicit capability gap), never throw out of the call.

**Files to touch:**
1. `mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/body/LanguageTagResolver.kt` (new) — pure-Kotlin, JVM-testable open resolver + unknown recording.
2. `mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/body/StreamingTts.kt` — use the resolver; wrap the synthesis loop so a failing organ reports to `FailureSurface` and the loop keeps serving (AC4).
3. `mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/OllamaModelBackend.kt` (+ possibly `ModelManager.kt`) — registry-aware name resolution with graceful degradation.

**Tests** in `com.jarvis.app.launchsurvival` (JVM): AC1(a) language-tag resolver never throws for `EN/en/en-US/ar-EG/""/xx-YY`; AC1(b) existing `ResidentTierLoadTest` drives production load path against `FakeOllama`; AC3 typed-turn returns generated text over the fake server; AC4 failing organ contained + recorded via `FailureSurface`.

**Docs (AC2/AC5/AC6):** audit via `vision_audit.sh`; `notes` must state where `"resident"` came from and why main warmed without this error, and list each process's constructed components; new incident file + `INDEX.md` row; REPO_FACTS.md lines.

**Risk noted:** this story's stored `verifyCommand` needs a working `GITHUB_TOKEN`; CI has been the only proof path. Token is set now (40 chars). I will implement fully, then verify via CI.

Let me start. First, the AC1(a) failing test and the resolver. Let me check `BodyTypes.TtsSegment` and `FailureReport` fields.

package com.jarvis.app.model

/**
 * A request to wake a model organ in a specific tier.
 *
 * @property organRole human-readable role of the waking organ (e.g. "reasoning", "voice")
 * @property tier the tier the organ wants to wake in
 * @property essential essential wakes must be served (a user turn under doubt is
 *   essential; a background prefetch is not). Non-essential wakes may be refused
 *   outright by low-battery policy; essential wakes degrade instead of failing bare.
 */
data class OrganWakeRequest(
    val organRole: String,
    val tier: ModelTier,
    val essential: Boolean = false
)

/** The governor's answer to an organ wake request. */
sealed interface AdmissionDecision {
    /** Admitted at the requested tier. */
    object ALLOW : AdmissionDecision

    /** Refused — no fallback (only for non-essential wakes that may be skipped). */
    object DENY : AdmissionDecision

    /** Admitted, but must serve from [lowerTier] instead (degraded cognition). */
    data class DEGRADE_TO(val lowerTier: ModelTier) : AdmissionDecision
}

/**
 * Per-tier resource budget for wake admission.
 *
 * A tier has headroom when the live snapshot has AT LEAST
 * [minAvailableMemoryMb] RAM free, CPU load no higher than [maxCpuLoadPercent],
 * thermal status no worse than [maxThermalLevel], and battery no lower than
 * [minBatteryPercent] (unless charging).
 */
data class TierResourceBudget(
    val tier: ModelTier,
    val minAvailableMemoryMb: Long = 512,
    val maxCpuLoadPercent: Double = 80.0,
    val maxThermalLevel: Int = 2,
    val minBatteryPercent: Int = 10
)

/**
 * ResourceGovernor — the admission authority gating every model-organ wake.
 *
 * Evaluates a [ResourceSnapshot] against per-tier budgets:
 *  - the [ModelTier.RESIDENT] tier is ALWAYS allowed (never downgraded)
 *  - RAM / CPU / thermal pressure returns [AdmissionDecision.DEGRADE_TO] the
 *    next-lower tier — the degraded-cognition ladder, never a bare denial
 *    without a fallback
 *  - low battery while not charging denies NON-essential wakes outright but
 *    still degrades ESSENTIAL ones rather than failing them bare
 *
 * Determinism: [admit] is a pure function of (request, snapshot); tests inject
 * a fake snapshot. The unwired default snapshot provider is an all-clear
 * placeholder — ModelManager wires the live [AndroidResourceSnapshot].
 */

Now I'll make `OllamaModelBackend.loadModel` resolve the requested name against the registry with degradation. Design:
- exact match → use as-is
- `name` + `:latest` present → use that
- requested `name:latest` where `name` is registered → use registered
- no match: degrade to the tier's registered model if any known tier model exists, else explicit capability gap (a structured exception, not thrown bare from the model call — but AC3 says "never throws out of the call").

AC3: "A registry that lacks the tier degrades or reports a gap and never throws out of the call." So `loadModel` must not throw; it should return a handle or signal a gap. But `ModelBackend.loadModel` returns `ModelHandle` (non-null) and the interface can't return null. Let me check the interface.

 }
 
+/**
+ * How the requested id was matched against [registered] — observable so a
+ * test can prove a tier name was RESOLVED (exact / ':latest' / degraded)
+ * rather than assumed, and so a genuine gap is named instead of thrown.
+ */
+data class ModelResolution(
+    val requestedId: String,
+    val resolvedId: String,
+    val match: Match,
+    val registered: List<String>
+) {
+    enum class Match { EXACT, LATEST_FORM, DEGRADED_TO_REGISTERED, EMPTY_REGISTRY }
+    val degraded: Boolean get() = match == Match.DEGRADED_TO_REGISTERED || match == Match.EMPTY_REGISTRY
+}
+
+/** The last name resolution performed by [loadModel] (null before any load). */
+@Volatile
+var lastResolution: ModelResolution? = null
+    private set
+
+/**
+ * Resolve [requested] against the names the server registers. Never throws:
+ * an unmatched request degrades to a registered model (preferring the same
+ * model family, then the sole registered model) and an empty registry
+ * yields an explicit EMPTY_REGISTRY resolution that names the gap.
+ */
+private fun resolveRegisteredModel(requested: String, registered: List<String>): ModelResolution {
+    if (registered.isEmpty()) {
+        return ModelResolution(requested, requested, ModelResolution.Match.EMPTY_REGISTRY, registered)
+    }
+    if (requested in registered) {
+        return ModelResolution(requested, requested, ModelResolution.Match.EXACT, registered)
+    }
+    // The ':latest' form: "jarvis-resident" -> "jarvis-resident:latest".
+    val withLatest = "$requested:latest"
+    if (withLatest in registered) {
+        return ModelResolution(requested, withLatest, ModelResolution.Match.LATEST_FORM, registered)
+    }
+    // A request that already carries ':latest' but whose base name is
+    // registered under a different tag.
+    val base = requested.substringBefore(':')
+    val sameFamily = registered.firstOrNull { it.substringBefore(':') == base }
+    if (sameFamily != null) {
+        return ModelResolution(requested, sameFamily, ModelResolution.Match.LATEST_FORM, registered)
+    }
+    // Degrade: prefer a model whose family the REQUESTER is a prefix/substring
+    // of (e.g. "resident" -> "jarvis-resident:latest"), then the first
+    // registered model. This is the degradation path, recorded as such.
+    val prefixMatch = registered.firstOrNull { candidate ->
+        val family = candidate.substringBefore(':')
+        family.contains(requested, ignoreCase = true) || requested.contains(family, ignoreCase = true)
+    } ?: registered.first()
+    return ModelResolution(requested, prefixMatch, ModelResolution.Match.DEGRADED_TO_REGISTERED, registered)
+}
+
+

     }

package com.jarvis.app

import android.app.Application
import androidx.lifecycle.Lifecycle
import androidx.lifecycle.LifecycleEventObserver
import androidx.lifecycle.LifecycleOwner
import androidx.lifecycle.ProcessLifecycleOwner

class JarvisApplication : Application() {
    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()
        JarvisEngine.init(this)

        // Process-scoped background/foreground for the Companion Core (plan
        // §4.3, audit R-A3). ProcessLifecycleOwner fires ON_STOP only when the
        // LAST activity stops — i.e. the whole process backgrounds — so a
        // rotation or a config change (which stop/start the single activity)
        // cannot spurious-suspend the Presence Engine, and an off-screen orb
        // keeps rendering in split-screen until the entire process leaves the
        // foreground. The per-activity Human Core endSession hook stays in
        // MainActivity (M-4), guarded against config changes.
        ProcessLifecycleOwner.get().lifecycle.addObserver(object : LifecycleEventObserver {
            override fun onStateChanged(source: LifecycleOwner, event: Lifecycle.Event) {
                val core = com.jarvis.app.companioncore.presence.CompanionCoreHolder.instance()
                when (event) {
                    Lifecycle.Event.ON_STOP -> core?.onBackground()
                    Lifecycle.Event.ON_START -> core?.onForeground()
                    else -> Unit
                }
            }
        })
    }
}

package com.jarvis.app.voice

import android.app.Service

private const val TAG = "VoiceService"

/**
 * The `:voice` process — the mutated environment that owns voice inference.
 *
 * R1 was a supervised placeholder (tone bytes proving the boundary). R2 hosts
 * the real providers:
 *
 *   - [VoiceProviderRegistry] (Sherpa/Kokoro + Android system TTS),
 *   - [VoiceProviderSelector] (profile → provider, deterministic),
 *   - [VoiceModelManager] (lazy download / admission / lifecycle),
 *   - [VoiceFailureController] (classified failures cross IPC to the host).
 *
 * Every native inference call is routed through [VoiceScheduler] so at most one
 * runs at a time (the same serialization discipline that prevented the old

Root cause found: `FakeOllama.kt` uses `com.sun.net.httpserver.HttpServer` (JDK's `jdk.httpserver` module), which is **not on the Android unit-test compile classpath**. The Android unit test runtime uses the `android.jar` stubs + Android toolchain, not the full JDK, so `com.sun.*` is unresolved.

I need a fake HTTP server that does not depend on `com.sun.net.httpserver`. Options: use a tiny raw `java.net.ServerSocket`-based HTTP responder (JDK `java.net` **is** available in Android unit tests) — that avoids `com.sun.*` entirely. Let me rewrite `FakeOllama` on a raw `ServerSocket` + a thread, parsing requests minimally.

[0m$ [0mcd /sdcard/jarvis-repo && sed -n '49,140p' mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/OllamaModelBackend.kt
override suspend fun loadModel(config: ModelBackendConfig): ModelHandle {
        val registered = try {
            ollama.listModels()
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            throw IOException(
                "Ollama server unreachable at 127.0.0.1:8080 — cannot load '${config.modelId}': ${e.message}"
            )
        }
        // LAUNCH-SURVIVAL AC3: names are RESOLVED, not assumed. The phone
        // registered `jarvis-resident:latest` and `jarvis-reasoning:latest`,
        // but the tier's default id was the literal "resident" — the old
        // `require(modelId in registered)` threw and took the :voice process
        // down. The requested id is now resolved against what the server
        // actually registers (exact name, then the ':latest' form, then a
        // degrade to a registered model), and a registry that lacks the tier
        // degrades instead of throwing out of the call.
        val resolution = resolveRegisteredModel(config.modelId, registered)
        lastResolution = resolution
        val handle = ModelHandle(id = idSeq.incrementAndGet(), modelId = resolution.resolvedId)
        synchronized(loadedHandles) { loadedHandles.add(handle) }
        loadCount++
        return handle
    }

    /**
     * How the requested id was matched against [registered] — observable so a
     * test can prove a tier name was RESOLVED (exact / ':latest' / degraded)
     * rather than assumed, and so a genuine gap is named instead of thrown.
     *
     * The outcome is DATA, not a closed set of forms: [resolvedId] is what the
     * load will actually use, [degraded] says whether the request was
     * substituted, and [basis] records why in plain words. A new resolution
     * strategy adds no enum member — it just resolves differently.
     */
    data class ModelResolution(
        val requestedId: String,
        val resolvedId: String,
        val degraded: Boolean,
        val basis: String,
        val registered: List<String>
    ) {
        /** True when the server registered nothing to resolve against. */
        val emptyRegistry: Boolean get() = registered.isEmpty()
    }

    /** The last name resolution performed by [loadModel] (null before any load). */
    @Volatile
    var lastResolution: ModelResolution? = null
        private set

    /**
     * Resolve [requested] against the names the server registers. Never throws:
     * an unmatched request degrades to a registered model (preferring the same
     * model family, then the sole registered model) and an empty registry
     * yields an explicit empty-registry resolution that names the gap.
     */
    private fun resolveRegisteredModel(requested: String, registered: List<String>): ModelResolution {
        if (registered.isEmpty()) {
            return ModelResolution(
                requestedId = requested,
                resolvedId = requested,
                degraded = true,
                basis = "empty registry: no model registered, request kept as the explicit gap",
                registered = registered
            )
        }
        if (requested in registered) {
            return ModelResolution(requested, requested, degraded = false, basis = "exact", registered = registered)
        }
        // The ':latest' form: "jarvis-resident" -> "jarvis-resident:latest".
        val withLatest = "$requested:latest"
        if (withLatest in registered) {
            return ModelResolution(requested, withLatest, degraded = false, basis = "':latest' form", registered = registered)
        }
        // A request that already carries ':latest' but whose base name is
        // registered under a different tag.
        val base = requested.substringBefore(':')
        val sameFamily = registered.firstOrNull { it.substringBefore(':') == base }
        if (sameFamily != null) {
            return ModelResolution(requested, sameFamily, degraded = false, basis = "same family under another tag", registered = registered)
        }
        // Degrade: prefer a model whose family the REQUESTER is a prefix/substring
        // of (e.g. "resident" -> "jarvis-resident:latest"), then the first
        // registered model. This is the degradation path, recorded as such.
        val prefixMatch = registered.firstOrNull { candidate ->
            val family = candidate.substringBefore(':')
            family.contains(requested, ignoreCase = true) || requested.contains(family, ignoreCase = true)
        }
        return if (prefixMatch != null) {
            ModelResolution(requested, prefixMatch, degraded = true, basis = "degraded to a registered family match", registered = registered)
        } else {
            ModelResolution(requested, registered.first(), degraded = true, basis = "degraded to the first registered model", registered = registered)
[0m

[0m$ [0mcd /sdcard/jarvis-repo && grep -n "override suspend fun generate" mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/OllamaModelBackend.kt; sed -n '/override suspend fun generate/,/^    }/p' mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/OllamaModelBackend.kt | head -60
145:    override suspend fun generate(handle: ModelHandle, prompt: String): String {
    override suspend fun generate(handle: ModelHandle, prompt: String): String {
        check(isLoaded(handle)) {
            "OllamaModelBackend.generate on an unloaded handle $handle — a handle is valid only after loadModel()"
        }
        val result = ollama.generate(
            GenerateRequest(
                messages = listOf(Message(role = MessageRole.USER, content = prompt)),
                maxTokens = 200,
                // REASONING-TIER-MODEL-GROUND-TRUTH-AND-WIRE: the /api/chat
                // request must target the handle's model — the ON_DEMAND_REASONING
                // handle carries jarvis-reasoning, so a tier-served generation is
                // genuinely answered by the distinct reasoning model, never by the
                // adapter-configured resident model.
                modelId = handle.modelId
            )
        )
        generateCount++
        check(result.finishReason != FinishReason.ERROR && result.content.isNotBlank()) {
            "Ollama generate failed for '${handle.modelId}': finishReason=${result.finishReason} content='${result.content}'"
        }
        return result.content
    }

fun noteTurnServe(handle: ModelHandle) {
        turnServeHandle.set(handle)
    }

    /** Release the per-turn booking — send() already consumes it at call time. */
    internal fun clearTurnServe() {
        turnServeHandle.set(null)

    /** Fire-and-forget conversation send against the active provider. */
    fun send(message: String) {
        val booked = turnServeHandle.getAndSet(null)?.takeIf { modelBackend.isLoaded(it) }
        scope.launch {
            attemptSend(message, attempt = 0, tierHandle = booked)
        }
    }

    private suspend fun attemptSend(message: String, attempt: Int, tierHandle: ModelHandle? = null) {
        _status.value =
            if (attempt == 0) "sending (local)" else "retrying local (attempt ${attempt + 1})"
        try {
            val result = if (tierHandle != null) {
                val content = modelBackend.generate(tierHandle, message)
                if (content.isNotBlank()) {
                    GenerateResult(content = content, finishReason = FinishReason.STOP)
                } else {
                    GenerateResult("", finishReason = FinishReason.ERROR)
                }
            } else {
                generate(
                    GenerateRequest(
                        messages = listOf(Message(MessageRole.USER, message)),
                        maxTokens = 200
                    )
                )
            }
            val content = result.content
            if (content.isNotBlank() && result.finishReason != FinishReason.ERROR) {
                _status.value = "ok (local)"
                _lastReply.value = content
            } else {
                if (attempt < MAX_RETRIES) {
                    delay(RETRY_DELAYS_MS.getOrElse(attempt) { RETRY_DELAYS_MS.last() })
                    attemptSend(message, attempt + 1, tierHandle)
                } else {
                    _status.value = "local brain unreachable after ${attempt + 1} attempts"
                    _lastReply.value = ""
                }
            }
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            if (attempt < MAX_RETRIES) {
                delay(RETRY_DELAYS_MS.getOrElse(attempt) { RETRY_DELAYS_MS.last() })
                attemptSend(message, attempt + 1, tierHandle)
            } else {
                _status.value =
                    "local brain unreachable after ${attempt + 1} attempts (${e.message})"
                _lastReply.value = ""
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Synchronous, short-timeout chat for the Human Core model port. Off-main
     * thread only; bounded by [timeoutMs]. Returns null when the active
     * provider cannot serve a short chat (heuristic/none, or unreachable) —
     * callers MUST fall back (§0.15 model port contract).
     */
    fun requestChat(
        messages: List<Pair<String, String>>,
        maxTokens: Int = 120,
        timeoutMs: Long = 5_000L
    ): String? = _activeProvider.value.requestChat(messages, maxTokens, timeoutMs)

    /**
     * Synchronous generation with memory context for the BodyCoordinator.
     * Includes retrieved memory items in the prompt for context-aware responses.
     */
    fun sendWithContext(message: String, memoryItems: List<com.jarvis.app.body.MemoryItem>): String {
        val contextText = if (memoryItems.isNotEmpty()) {
            "\n\n[Context from memory]:\n" + memoryItems.map { "- ${it.content}" }.joinToString("\n")
        } else {
            ""
        }
        val fullMessage = message + contextText

        // Use the synchronous requestChat path with a longer timeout
        return requestChat(
            messages = listOf(Pair("user", fullMessage)),
            maxTokens = 200,
            timeoutMs = 10_000L
        ) ?: "I'm having trouble thinking right now. Could you try again?"

[0m$ [0mcd /sdcard/jarvis-repo && sed -n '68,110p' mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/adapters/OllamaAdapter.kt; echo "=== sendRequest ==="; grep -n "fun sendRequest\|endpoint\|/api/" mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/adapters/OllamaAdapter.kt
override suspend fun generate(request: GenerateRequest): GenerateResult = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
        val response = sendRequest(request, stream = false)

    override fun stream(request: GenerateRequest) = flow {
        val response = sendRequest(request, stream = true)
        val source = response.body?.source() ?: return@flow

        var accumulated = ""
        var usage: TokenUsage? = null

        source.use { bufferedSource ->
            while (true) {
                val line = bufferedSource.readUtf8Line() ?: break
                if (line.isNotBlank()) {
                    try {
                        val json = JSONObject(line)
                        val response = json.optString("response", "")
                        if (response.isNotEmpty()) {
                            accumulated += response
                            emit(StreamEvent.Token(response, accumulated))
                        }
                        if (json.getBoolean("done")) {
                            val promptTokens = json.optInt("prompt_eval_count", 0)
                            val completionTokens = json.optInt("eval_count", 0)
                            usage = TokenUsage(promptTokens, completionTokens, promptTokens + completionTokens)
                            emit(StreamEvent.Done(usage))
                            break
                        }
                    } catch (e: Exception) {
                        emit(StreamEvent.Error("Parse error: ${e.message}"))
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }.flowOn(Dispatchers.IO)

[0m$ [0mcd /sdcard/jarvis-repo && sed -n '247,300p' mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/adapters/OllamaAdapter.kt; echo "=== parseGenerateResponse ==="; grep -n "fun parseGenerateResponse" mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/adapters/OllamaAdapter.kt
private fun sendRequest(request: GenerateRequest, stream: Boolean): okhttp3.Response {
        val messages = JSONArray()
        request.messages.forEach { msg ->
            val obj = JSONObject()
            obj.put("role", msg.role.name.lowercase())
            obj.put("content", msg.content)
            messages.put(obj)
        }

        val body = JSONObject()
        body.put("model", request.modelId ?: currentModel)
        body.put("messages", messages)
        body.put("stream", stream)
        body.put("options", JSONObject().apply {
            put("temperature", request.temperature)
            put("top_p", request.topP)
            put("num_predict", request.maxTokens)
        })

        return client.newCall(
            Request.Builder()
                .url("$baseUrl/api/chat")
                .post(body.toString().toRequestBody(jsonMediaType))
                .build()
        ).execute()
    }

    private fun parseGenerateResponse(response: okhttp3.Response): GenerateResult {
        val body = response.body?.string() ?: return GenerateResult("", finishReason = com.jarvis.app.model.FinishReason.ERROR)
        if (!response.isSuccessful) {
            return GenerateResult("", finishReason = com.jarvis.app.model.FinishReason.ERROR)
        }
        try {
            val json = JSONObject(body)
            val message = json.getJSONObject("message")
            val content = message.getString("content")
            return GenerateResult(content, finishReason = com.jarvis.app.model.FinishReason.STOP)
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            return GenerateResult("", finishReason = com.jarvis.app.model.FinishReason.ERROR)
        }
    }
}=== parseGenerateResponse ===

[0m$ [0mcd /sdcard/jarvis-repo && sed -n '405,470p' mobile/app/src/main/java/com/jarvis/app/model/ModelManager.kt
private suspend fun loadOnce(
        tier: ModelTier,
        modelId: String,
        providerType: ModelProviderType?
    ): ModelHandle {
        // ADAPTER-MANIFEST: an adapter whose training sources include a forgotten
        // memory must not enter memory at all. The check sits BEFORE any load
        // state is claimed, so a refused adapter consumes no cache slot and
        // cannot slip in through the single-flight path either.
        val gate = adapterLoadGate
        if (gate != null && !gate.mayLoad(modelId)) {
            throw com.jarvis.app.memory.provenance.TaintedAdapterRefused(modelId)
        }
        when (val outcome = beginLoad(tier)) {
            is LoadOutcome.Cached -> {
                synchronized(lifecycleLock) { lastUsedMs[tier] = clock() }
                return outcome.handle
            }
            is LoadOutcome.InFlight -> return outcome.deferred.await()
            is LoadOutcome.Lead -> {
                val handle = try {
                    modelBackend.loadModel(
                        ModelBackendConfig(
                            modelId = modelId,
                            providerType = providerType ?: _activeProvider.value.providerType
                        )
                    )
                } catch (e: Exception) {
                    synchronized(lifecycleLock) { inflight.remove(tier) }
                    outcome.deferred.completeExceptionally(e)
                    throw e
                }
                synchronized(lifecycleLock) {
                    cachedByTier[tier] = handle
                    lastUsedMs[tier] = clock()
                    inflight.remove(tier)
                }
                outcome.deferred.complete(handle)
                return handle
            }
        }
    }

    private sealed interface LoadOutcome {
        data class Cached(val handle: ModelHandle) : LoadOutcome
        data class InFlight(val deferred: CompletableDeferred<ModelHandle>) : LoadOutcome
        data class Lead(val deferred: CompletableDeferred<ModelHandle>) : LoadOutcome
    }

    // Start the background cooldown sweep (unless tests inject a manual clock).
    init {
        if (autoSweep) {
            scope.launch {
                while (true) {
                    delay(cooldownMs)
                    try {
                        runCooldownSweep()
                    } catch (e: Exception) {
                        // Sweep is best-effort; keep the loop alive for the next pass.
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

* List the models the Ollama server actually registers (real GET /api/tags).
     * Throws when the server is unreachable or answers non-2xx. This is the
     * ground-truth probe a caller uses to confirm a model is really there —
     * never a filesystem guess.

    suspend fun listModels(): List<String> = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
        val request = Request.Builder()
            .url("$baseUrl/api/tags")
            .get()
            .build()
        client.newCall(request).execute().use { response ->
            if (!response.isSuccessful) {
                throw IOException("listModels failed: HTTP ${response.code}")
            }
            val json = JSONObject(response.body?.string() ?: "{}")
            val array = json.optJSONArray("models") ?: JSONArray()
            val names = mutableListOf<String>()
            for (i in 0 until array.length()) {
                array.getJSONObject(i).optString("name", "").takeIf { it.isNotBlank() }?.let { names.add(it) }
            }
            names
        }
    }

## Summary

Fixed both fatal launch crashes on the Realme and proved it in CI.

**Root causes & fixes**
- **CRASH A** (`valueOf("EN")` → `IllegalArgumentException` on the TTS IO worker): new pure `LanguageTagResolver` + `LanguageRouter.resolveLanguageTag` seam. External tags now resolve openly (never `valueOf`); unknown/empty/differently-cased degrade to the default voice and are recorded as unknown. `StreamingTts.synthesizeSegment:306` uses it.
- **CRASH B** (`require("resident" in registered)`): `OllamaModelBackend.loadModel` now **resolves** the requested name against the server registry (exact → `:latest` → same-family → degrade → explicit `EMPTY_REGISTRY` gap) and never throws for a missing tier. The literal `"resident"` originates in `ModelManager.defaultModelId` (`ModelManager.kt:287-296`) when the active provider config is blank.
- **Containment:** `StreamingTts.startSynthesisLoop` wraps each segment, reports through `FailureSurface`, keeps serving.

**Proof (CI, HEAD `e65b4ab`, run 39527010088):** `8 matched test(s), all passed` + `build status success`. `vision_audit` over the story range = **0 deviations** (I replaced the `Match` enum with data-bearing `ModelResolution` to clear the one `closed_type` finding).

**Bookkeeping:** incident `2026-10-18_closed_enum_assumed_name.md` + INDEX row; REPO_FACTS lessons (per-check prevention lines + the AC5 one-body finding for ONE-2-COMPOSITION); story `notes` filled with per-process `file:line` evidence; `passes:true`; progress entry.

Next pending story: **ONE-2-COMPOSITION** (priority 9, still `passes:false` — committed, needs its own CI proof).

      "title": "The shared cognitive workspace: claims instead of named wires",
      "description": "This is the real fix for the isolation problem this project keeps re-catching in code review - no subsystem may read another by name. Add com.jarvis.app.cognition.workspace: a Claim (id, kind, payload, confidence 0..1, sourceOrgan, personId nullable - reserved now even though full multi-person scoping is a later gate, so this schema never needs a breaking migration for it, decayRate, createdAt, supersedes nullable) and a Workspace interface (publish(claim), claims(kind, personId=null), current(kind, personId=null) returning the highest-confidence live claim, decay/tick()). Existing hardcoded seams that already do this job informally (ContextWindowAssembler's deterministic union, the mental-state seam) get audited first and their real callers migrated to publish/read through Workspace instead of a direct reference - do not build a second, parallel state store alongside them. Any claim whose kind is tagged as a PREDICTION must carry a baseline field (what the naive most-common-answer would have been) and a wasCorrect nullable outcome field, set once the real next turn confirms or contradicts it - no predictive claim is allowed to exist in the schema without a way to later prove whether it beat guessing.
